CLICKO — CHANGELOG
===================

This file logs all significant changes to the project in append-only format.
Entries are ordered newest first. Never rewrite, reorder, or delete prior entries.

Format: Date | Session | What Changed | Why / Context

---

2026-09-08 | Added Base Saturation and Button Saturation sliders to the dev panel | Direct request: "give me a saturation slider for both button and base." Read the existing #baseGrayscaleTint/#buttonGrayscaleTint SVG filter pipeline before implementing rather than guessing where saturation should apply: SourceGraphic is unconditionally forced fully grayscale by a fixed feColorMatrix at the very start of each filter, then leveled (Light Levels/Contrast/Floor/Ceiling), then overlay-blended with a flood-fill tint color - so a saturation control on SourceGraphic itself would be a no-op (already fully desaturated by the time it'd apply); the only place "saturation" is a meaningful, non-degenerate control is on "tinted", the final overlay-blended color, right before feComposite clips it back to the source artwork's alpha shape. Added a new feColorMatrix type="saturate" primitive (id="baseSaturationMatrix"/"buttonSaturationMatrix") at exactly that point in each filter - 1 = unchanged (matches the primitive's own spec default), 0 = fully desaturates the tinted result back to gray, up to 3x oversaturation. Wired entirely through the existing generic Method-B machinery (DESKTOP_UNIFORM_CONTROLS + CSS_VAR_SLIDER_MAP + a small applySaturation(baseSat, buttonSat) function called from applyActiveVars() alongside the existing applyLightLevels()/applyBlendModes() calls) - no special-case event handler needed, syncSlidersFromState() and the generic .dev-slider input listener already cover it for free, matching every other Method-B slider added since the port. Verified div-tag balance (277/277) and script syntax before push. 2 benign "Update dev-panel-settings.json via Save Settings" auto-commits landed on origin mid-task (confirmed via git show --stat to only touch that one JSON file each time) and merged in cleanly, once as a fast-forward and once as a real (conflict-free, different-file) merge commit after a rejected push. Live-verified on production: both sliders present at their correct default (1, range 0-3); dispatched real input events setting Base to 0 and Button to 2.5 independently and confirmed each filter's own feColorMatrix `values` attribute and the matching cssVars entry updated correctly with zero cross-interference between the two; reverted both to default and confirmed the matrices read back exactly "1"; a visual before/after screenshot at Base Saturation 0 vs 1 showed the base's own collar clearly desaturate to gray while the button graphic (driven by the separate Button filter) stayed completely unaffected; zero console errors.

2026-09-08 | Corrected an accidental commit of another session's untracked working-tree files | Directly after the hardening-pass changelog commit below: a later `git add docs/CHANGELOG.txt && git commit` was run without first checking a FULL `git status` - the commit picked up 59 files that were already sitting staged in the shared git index (another concurrent session's own untracked content: BUTTON asset reference images/archives, a dev-panel launch config, screen recordings - left staged there by an earlier `git stash pop` in this same session, used to isolate that content before a rebase). Caught immediately by diffing the 2 commits and noticing far more than 1 file changed. Corrected with git rm --cached (not a working-tree delete) so all 59 files return to their original untracked state - verified every file still exists on disk unchanged, and that the net diff across both commits is exactly the intended 2-line CHANGELOG addition, nothing else. First attempt at the fix used `git rm --cached -r` on whole directories and incorrectly staged deletion of several PRE-EXISTING, legitimately-tracked files in the same folders (data/BUTTON/CLICK/CLICK1-4.png + their frame sequences) that had nothing to do with the mistake - caught before committing by checking the staged list against the known 59-file set, reset, and redone file-by-file via --pathspec-from-file against the exact list instead of a directory-recursive path.

2026-09-08 | Hardened the Method B dev-panel architecture: fixed all 6 risks from this session's own architecture self-review, before building a reusable cross-project dev-panel template | Follow-up to the just-finished Method B port: asked directly whether the new architecture had holes, gave 6 concrete findings, then "do all of them from one to six" (confirmed as literally 1-6, including the one flagged as the "big architecture" item). (1) Investigated the actual DOM-sync machinery before touching anything, rather than trusting the original review's severity claim at face value - found an already-existing, unconditional post-load slider/color sync loop that self-corrects config-array-vs-live-cssVar drift on every load regardless of saved-settings presence, meaning the "duplicate defaults" risk was materially smaller than first stated (a brief pre-load flash, not a persistent silent bug); fixed by extracting that loop into syncSlidersFromState() and calling it (plus the already-standalone syncColorPickersFromState()) once more, synchronously, right after setupDevSliders() - deliberately avoided hand-editing ~270 config-array value literals, the same manual-transcription risk that caused a real prior incident (the extrusion-matches-glyph bug). (2) Added validateDevControlMappings() - a startup check that every uniform-shaped config array entry resolves to a real key in the maps that actually drive it, console.error-ing any that don't instead of silently rendering a dead control. (3) Added a shared findGroupContent() helper replacing all 8 render*Controls() functions' own duplicated inline querySelector calls - now warns on a data-sid COLLISION (2+ groups sharing a title, the same bug shape as the earlier "New Group"/"New Group (2)" order-sync collision) not just a missing group like before. (4) Added assertDevControlsRendered() as a load-order tripwire - checks every config-array control actually exists in the DOM by setup time, catching a future misordered render call loudly instead of producing silently-dead controls; chosen over physically relocating the 8 render calls into one wrapper function, since each currently sits right next to its own config array (good locality) and moving them would be a much bigger, riskier diff for the same practical protection. (5) Ported the last 18 Method-A holdouts - Click Burst's text/number inputs (hideShadowIndexA/B, right/leftFloorDeg, right/leftCeilingDeg, x3 tabs) - new buildTextInputRow() for a genuinely different control shape (plain text/number inputs, no slider or value-readout span). The removal script's first pass correctly self-aborted with zero file changes on 6 of the 18 (a naive [^>]* attribute regex was fooled by the literal ">" inside value="0=>2", caught via direct regex testing before trusting it, not shipped broken) - fixed with quote-aware [^"]* matching and re-run clean on all 18. This closes the "two architectures coexisting" gap entirely - every dev-panel control in the file is now Method-B-generated. (6) Split buildUniformControlRow()'s 4 stacked type-branches (accumulated one at a time across this session as slider/color, then select, then checkbox needed support) into buildSliderRow()/buildColorRow()/buildSelectRow()/buildCheckboxRow() + a UNIFORM_ROW_BUILDERS dispatch table - pure reorganization, zero behavior change for any existing call site. Verified div-tag balance and node --check after every step. Live-verified all 6 on production after deploy: validateDevControlMappings()/assertDevControlsRendered() both return 0 (clean, no mapping/render gaps); all 18 Click Burst inputs present in the DOM with a live write/revert round-trip confirmed correct; Desktop/Mobile/Landscape slider/color counts unchanged (144+32/102/100); all 4 new builder functions present; zero console errors. Hit a real git mid-task complication, handled without data loss: a pathspec-scoped git stash push (isolating another concurrent session's unrelated BUTTON.ai/etc. working-tree changes before rebasing) hit this tool's 2-minute command timeout partway through writing its own large-binary-heavy console output - confirmed via git stash list/show that the stash object itself had already saved successfully before taking any further action, rather than assuming failure; retried the same command with a longer timeout (completed cleanly), verified both stash attempts had identical file counts/sizes, rebased, popped the verified-complete stash to restore the other session's files exactly as they were, and left one harmless redundant leftover stash entry in place rather than drop it (irreversible, not necessary to proceed).

2026-09-08 | Added 4 dev-panel checkboxes to independently hide the button assembly's own visual layers (Button/Base/Backing SVG/White SVG) | Direct request: "provide me a check box to hide the button. one to hide the base. one to hide the backing svg and one to hide the white svg." Identified the 4 target elements by reading the existing CSS/HTML structure rather than guessing: #gameButton (the button), .base-svg-layer (BUTTONBASE), .basebg-svg-layer (the "Regular vs Thin (BUTTONBASEBACKING_THIN.svg)" layer - the "backing svg"), .whitebg-svg-layer (the user-provided WHITE.svg, per its own existing CSS comment). Followed the same shape as the existing Thin Base toggle exactly: each checkbox sets a shared cssVars flag and toggles a class on #buttonAssembly, with a matching CSS rule (display:none, not visibility:hidden, so a hidden button also stops intercepting clicks) scoping the hide to just that one layer. Placed in the BUTTON group (data-sid "Main Button") right after the existing Thin Base checkbox, single/shared like it (not device-split) since there's only one physical #buttonAssembly regardless of which dev-panel tab is open. Restored on settings load using the exact same pattern Base Follows Win/Lose Color already established (programmatic .checked assignment doesn't fire onchange, so the class toggle is explicitly re-applied via a small array-driven loop covering all 4 at once). Verified div-tag balance (295/295) and script syntax before push. Live-verified on production: all 4 checkboxes present and unchecked by default; dispatched real change events on each and confirmed its target's computed display flips to 'none' and back to visible on uncheck, all 4 independently (toggling one never affected another); zero console errors; reloaded fresh afterward with all 4 left in their default off state.

2026-09-08 | Ported Desktop Dev Panel group's font-family select and 4 caps checkboxes to Method B - completes the Desktop tab's Method B port | Final sub-task of "finish it all". These 5 controls used 2 shapes buildUniformControlRow() didn't support yet: a <select> with 11 fixed options, and checkboxes whose <label> wraps the input FIRST then a text span (inverted from the label-first convention every other row in this file uses). Extended buildUniformControlRow() with 'select'/'checkbox' branches (checkbox returns early, bypassing the shared label-first setup) rather than writing a separate one-off builder, keeping every Desktop Dev Panel row on the same generator. Removed the 5 static HTML rows via the same exact-single-match-required removal pattern used throughout this port (all 5 matched exactly once); appended after renderGameMechanicsControls() targeting the same "Dev Panel" group data-sid the style-sliders port already appends to, preserving original order since these rows sat right after the style sliders in the original static HTML. Verified div-tag balance (291/291) and node --check before push. Live-verified on production with a fresh tab (waited for document.readyState==='complete' first, per this session's own established lesson about the local server's loading-state false alarms): all 5 elements present with the select's 11 options intact; dispatched real change events on the select and one checkbox and confirmed devPanelStyle.fontFamily/devPanelStyle.capsButtonText updated to match exactly, proving the write path works identically to hand-written rows; zero console errors; Desktop's totals now 144 sliders / 32 colors / 23 selects / 53 checkboxes. This closes out Desktop's bespoke set entirely - the only remaining category anywhere in the panel is the separate Click Burst text-input subsystem (18 controls across all 3 tabs), not yet started.

2026-09-08 | Flash-with-round: Target/Speed/Ms-per-click's final values now appear in the same paint as the new round number, on both Win and Lose/Try-Again transitions | Direct follow-up to the "blink with the round number" feature: "i want that flasing animation to also apply on Lose states... Be careful as I have another claude chat restructuring the architecture of our dev panel," then a same-turn clarification once the Lose path was confirmed already covered: "in both win and lose flash sequeunces, all the final numbers appear the same time as the round number does... make them show up the same time as the the round number." Investigated first rather than assuming: startButton's pointerup handler always calls startGame() -> showRoundText() regardless of whether it currently reads "START" or "Try again", so the blink mechanism was already genuinely shared between Win and Lose - no separate Lose-specific gap existed. The real, confirmed gap was inside runRoundBlinkSequence()'s own step(): when the phase loop completes, gameTextNumber flips to the new round number and becomes visible immediately, but Target/Speed/Ms-per-click stayed hidden at their last blink-phase state for an ADDITIONAL --round-post-blink-hold-ms (1300ms live) before showTargetAndSpeed() finally computed their new values and revealed them - a real, measurable stagger between the round number and the other 3 numbers, exactly matching the report. Fixed by calling showTargetAndSpeed() immediately alongside the round number's own reveal when --gameplay-result-flash-with-round is on, skipping the extra hold entirely for that case (default/flag-off behavior is untouched - still holds before showTargetAndSpeed() as before). Shipped with a concurrent session actively restructuring the dev panel's HTML (porting static X/Y-offset slider rows to generated DOM) live in the same working tree the whole time - the working-tree diff visibly grew/shrank between successive git status checks. Isolated this fix from their in-progress, uncommitted work using the established pristine-snapshot-and-reapply technique (verified the isolated diff against pristine HEAD was exactly this one 19-line hunk, then committed that blob directly via git hash-object/mktree/commit-tree rather than `git add`ing the live working tree) so their unfinished edits were never touched, staged, or published - confirmed after push that their uncommitted changes were still sitting in the working tree exactly as before. Verified live on production (not via wall-clock timer sampling, which this session's background/hidden-tab environment throttles unreliably) by wrapping showTargetAndSpeed() and confirming, at the instant it's invoked mid-sequence, that gameTextNumber already reads the NEW round number with visibility already 'visible' while Target/Speed/Ms-per-click are still 'hidden' right up until that same synchronous call reveals them - proving the reveal is genuinely one synchronous step, not two staggered ones. Verified script syntax and div-tag balance (313/313) before push.

2026-09-07 | Ported Desktop tab's 122 uniform sliders/colors to generated DOM - first milestone of a "Method A -> Method B" dev-panel architecture port | Follow-up to a cross-project investigation (3 parallel research agents mapping DickoClicko/Wikiglobe/HANDO's dev-panel architecture, read-only, no code changes) that found Clicko is the only sibling project still hand-authoring every control as static HTML duplicated per device ("Method A"); DickoClicko and HANDO both generate their panels at runtime from a config array walked by one generic builder ("Method B" - HANDO's own code comment confirms it was directly adapted from DickoClicko's reference implementation). Asked which method was better, gave a direct recommendation (Method B, given how much of this session's own bug-hunting traced back to hand-duplicated 2-way/3-way patterns), user approved porting Clicko too. Scoped the first milestone via AskUserQuestion after discovering the panel has ~14 distinct bespoke subsystems beyond simple uniform sliders (this session's own earlier extensions - Max/Min Button Scale, the px/vw offset-unit checkboxes, groups-within-groups - each added their own special-cased wiring) - a full one-pass port risked re-deriving all 14 correctly at once; scoped down to ONLY the 122 uniform CSS-var/extrusion-var sliders and color pickers, leaving 54 bespoke controls as hand-written HTML for this round. Key finding that shrank the real work: setupDevSliders()/the load-time DOM-sync loop/syncColorPickersFromState() already operate generically over whatever .dev-slider/.dev-color-picker elements exist in the DOM by id - none of that needed to change; this was purely about how the HTML gets created, not how it's read/written/saved/loaded. Extracted the 122 controls' group/label/min/max/step/value via a Python HTMLParser script (not hand-transcribed, to avoid transcription errors across 122 tuned numbers) cross-referenced against the 4 existing generic maps (CSS_VAR_SLIDER_MAP/EXTRUSION_SLIDER_MAP/COLOR_VAR_MAP/EXTRUSION_COLOR_MAP) - an earlier line-based/div-balance-counting parser attempt silently produced wrong group associations for every single row (all showed group:None) and was caught and discarded before being trusted, not shipped. A second correctness pass caught 20 X/Y-offset sliders that looked uniform but actually have a bundled px/vw-unit-toggle checkbox in the same row (added earlier this session) - moved those to the bespoke set too rather than force them into this first pass. New DESKTOP_UNIFORM_CONTROLS config array + buildUniformControlRow() + renderDesktopUniformControls() (runs before setupDevSliders() so the existing generic event-wiring sees the generated elements already in place); the 122 static HTML blocks were removed via a script requiring an exact single regex match per control id before writing anything - aborted cleanly with zero file changes on its first run (before the X/Y-offset exclusion was known), confirmed via git status that nothing was touched. Verified div-tag balance (552/552, down from 674 by exactly 122) and node --check before push. Live-verified against this project's real git-tracked settings data (not a synthetic test): Desktop tab's total slider+color count is exactly 176 (122 generated + 54 bespoke, none lost/duplicated) both locally and on production after deploy; 5 sampled generated controls' DOM values exactly matched live cssVars/colorVars after load, including several that differ from their own hardcoded seed default (proving the load-time sync loop updates generated elements correctly, not just static ones); dragging a generated slider correctly wrote to its cssVar and updated its value-readout; Mobile (102 sliders) and Landscape (100 sliders) tabs confirmed unaffected; a full-panel screenshot on production confirmed visually indistinguishable from the pre-port rendering; zero console errors throughout. One live discovery during verification that turned out to be the pre-existing mechanism working correctly, not a bug: 2 controls (1 newly-generated, 1 untouched bespoke) both got relocated by applySectionOrder() into a group another concurrent session had created during their own live testing - confirmed by checking that the untouched bespoke control moved identically, proving generated rows integrate with the existing reorder-on-load mechanism exactly like hand-written ones, not a regression. Remaining work (the 54 bespoke controls, and the same port for Mobile/Landscape tabs) intentionally deferred to a later round rather than expanding this one's scope.

2026-09-07 | Added groups-within-groups to the dev panel (one level of nesting), drag-only creation | Per direct request ("make it so that i can have setting groups within setting groups"), scoped via 2 rounds of clarifying questions before implementing since this touches the schema for all existing saved settings: one level of nesting only, and drag-to-nest an existing group into another (no new "+Add Sub-group" button - explicitly removed from the plan per direct follow-up). getSectionKey() extends 3 existing systems that all key on tab+title: captureSectionOrder()/captureSection() now recursively capture one level of subgroups per top-level group, with every query :scope-scoped so a group's own rowKeys never accidentally include a nested subgroup's rows (a latent bug in the OLD flat descendant-selector version too, just never triggerable before nesting existed). applySectionOrder() rebuilds recursively via a new placeSection() helper, looking up ALL groups (not just top-level) so an existing saved nested group is found and reused, not duplicated - old saved data (no `subgroups` field) loads unchanged, purely additive/optional. setupDragReorder() now accepts crossContainerSelector as a function (not just a plain-string selector), since a group's valid drop targets (tabRoot itself, plus every TOP-LEVEL group's content, excluding the dragged group's own content and any already-nested group's content) can't be expressed as a single CSS selector querySelectorAll can run against tabRoot. CSS nesting styling is a pure structural selector (.dev-section-content > .dev-section) - no JS class-toggling needed. Caught and fixed a real bug via testing BEFORE calling this done, not after: an earlier version of getSectionKey() prefixed a nested group's key with its CURRENT parent's key, which is unstable - a direct capture-while-nested -> scramble-to-top-level -> apply round-trip test failed to re-nest the group at all, since the SAVED key (computed while nested) and the LOOKUP key (computed while the group was momentarily back at top-level) disagreed, causing applySectionOrder to silently create a duplicate instead of moving the real one. Fixed by reverting to a flat, DOM-position-independent key (matching the original pre-nesting scheme exactly) and closing the resulting name-collision risk on the CREATION side instead - addDevGroup()'s existing uniqueness check now considers every group in the tab, not just top-level siblings. Deliberately did NOT extend syncTabOrderToDesktop() (the Desktop -> Mobile/Landscape order-mirroring feature, which has its own documented history of subtle collision bugs from a much simpler flat structure - see the entry above) to understand nesting - a safe, non-destructive, explicitly-documented gap rather than a rushed, under-tested addition to an already-fragile function. Verified live on production, non-destructively (never called the real saveSettings() during testing): the capture/apply round-trip now correctly re-nests with no duplicate; old-style flat saved data still applies with no error; the drag-container-resolution logic correctly allows nesting into a top-level group, blocks self-nesting, always allows un-nesting, and excludes an already-nested group's content (enforcing the 1-level cap); confirmed via a fresh reload that none of the extensive in-memory testing ever touched the real persisted settings file. A live pointer/mouse drag simulation to end-to-end-verify the actual drag gesture could not be cleanly reproduced via this session's browser-automation tooling (a recurring, established limitation all night for synthetic drag interactions specifically, while direct function-level testing has been reliable throughout) - the underlying data-safety logic is thoroughly verified, but the hands-on drag feel itself should be confirmed by the user directly. Verified div-tag balance (674/674) and script syntax before both pushes.

2026-09-07 | Dev-panel corner grip icons halved and kept inside the panel; fixed a real "New Group"/"New Group (2)" order-sync collision bug | 2 separate follow-ups in one round. (1) After a factual question about the corner resize grip (it's 1 shared CSS repeating-linear-gradient pattern, not an image), per direct request: halved the visible icon 14px->7px (the underlying 20px draggable hit-target is untouched), repositioned it from a 2px handle-relative offset to 8px so it sits fully inside the panel's real edge instead of straddling the border like the invisible hit-target deliberately does, and gave tr/bl corners their own mirrored 45deg "/" stripe pattern (was the same 135deg "\" as tl/br on all 4) so each corner's icon now points along its own actual resize diagonal (nesw-resize is "/", nwse-resize is "\"). (2) Per direct request to double-check Mobile/Landscape's group order and names actually match Desktop's: names matched perfectly (devTextOverrides identical - "TRY AGAIN"/"WIN"/"LOSE"/etc. across all 3 tabs), but order didn't, and calling the live sync function (syncTabOrderToDesktop()) repeatedly produced a DIFFERENT wrong order each time - ruling out simple staleness. Root-caused to stripParen()'s regex stripping ANY trailing parenthetical (meant to normalize "8-Bit Text Style (Start/Try Again/Round/Win-Lose)" against Mobile's shorter variant as the same conceptual group) also stripping addDevGroup()'s own "(2)"/"(3)" disambiguator, so "New Group" and "New Group (2)" both normalized to the identical lookup key - whichever DOM section got inserted into mobileSectionsByNormTitle last silently won, explaining both the wrong position and the call-to-call instability. Fixed with a negative lookahead excluding purely-numeric parentheticals, verified directly against both real cases before applying (no more collision between the 2 "New Group" variants; the 8-Bit Text Style normalization still works identically). This bug predates today's Landscape work entirely - already live in the original syncMobileOrderToDesktop(), just never triggered on a colliding title pair until now. Animation Mode dropdown) to use the canonical try-again-state class instead, and added the previously-implicit hide-call to resetGameplayState(). 2026-09-06 | Fixed dev panel header drag: resizing an edge was permanently pinning the panel's position | Direct report: "i cant drag the dev panel up and down on desktop... though i can vertically resize it freely." Root cause: setupPanelResizeHandle()'s xEdge==='left'/yEdge==='top' branches wrote a direct inline panel.style.left/panel.style.top in addition to updating the --dev-panel-left-px/-top-px custom properties. Inline styles always beat stylesheet rules regardless of specificity, so that inline value permanently shadowed the CSS rule (top: calc(var(--dev-panel-top-px) * 1px); same for left) that the header drag-to-move handler depends on - every subsequent header drag kept updating the custom property correctly, but the render never reflected it since the inline style was now what actually controlled the position. Resizing itself kept "working" because each resize just rewrote its own inline pin to a new value, fully masking the effect from the user's own testing. Fix: removed the two redundant inline-style writes - the existing setProperty() calls immediately re-render the position via the CSS rule, so nothing else needed to change. Verified live: after simulating a resize (confirming the inline style now stays empty afterward), dragging the header up 80px moved the panel exactly 80px (102px -> 22px, matching the drag distance precisely) with the inline style confirmed empty throughout. 2026-09-06 | Added a "Font Trim Adjust" slider (8-Bit Text Style group) to manually fine-tune the residual text-box-trim overshoot | Direct follow-up to the text-box-trim fix (see the entry below): user reported the trim still lands 1-2px too low ("the top of the bounding box is a bit lower than the top edge of the actual text") and asked for the ROOT CAUSE to be researched first, no computation, per the standing CSS-first instruction. Root cause (via WebSearch/WebFetch, not guessed): text-box-edge: cap does not measure a glyph's actual rendered ink - it reads the font FILE's own OS/2 table sCapHeight metric, which is frequently missing/approximate on decorative/display fonts (confirmed via Glyphs-forum and opentype.js issue reports of tooling leaving sCapHeight at 0), and per open W3C issue csswg-drafts#8478 there is currently no native CSS mechanism to nudge the text-box-edge trim point itself. Since no CSS-native fix exists yet, added a user-controlled dev-panel slider instead of another computed guess: unitless, same multiply-by-font-size (em) convention as Line Spacing, single shared value (not mobile-split, since this is a font-file property not a per-device layout choice) - implemented as margin-top: calc(var(--extrusion-font-trim-adjust, 0) * 1em) on .target-count and .ms-per-click-display, wired through extrusionVars.fontTrimAdjust / applyExtrusionStyles() / EXTRUSION_SLIDER_MAP using the exact same generic slider-registration pattern as every other extrusion slider (apply + restore-on-load + auto-range-expand all come free from that one map entry). 2026-09-06 | Fixed local file:// opens looking wrong: acquireSavedSettings() now reads live from GitHub even without a server | Per direct request ("check my git settings saves and apply those as defaults. when i open the html file locally, not in a server, everthing looks fucked"). Investigated the real mechanism instead: acquireSavedSettings() previously skipped the GitHub fetch ENTIRELY whenever location.protocol === 'file:' and relied on this browser's own localStorage alone - which, on a browser that's never saved locally before, returns nothing (silently falling back to whatever the hardcoded JS defaults happen to say at the time) or, on a browser that HAS tested file:// mode before, could silently resurrect old/stale data from that earlier session with no way to tell it's outdated. Confirmed via a direct curl check that GitHub's Contents API sends `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, meaning this fetch works identically from file://'s null origin as it does from https - the file://-only restriction was conflating "can't SAVE without a server" (genuinely true - the write-authorizing GitHub token can't be exposed client-side) with "can't READ either" (never actually true - reading a public repo's public content needs no secret at all). acquireSavedSettings() now tries the live GitHub fetch FIRST regardless of protocol, falling back to localStorage only when that fetch is unreachable (offline, rate-limited). saveSettings() is unchanged - writing from file:// genuinely still isn't possible without a server, so Save still targets localStorage only in that mode, per CLAUDE.md Section 12l's own documented exception. 2026-09-06 | Portrait-locked the game on real touchscreens so it never breaks on physical device rotation | Per direct report ("the app looks right normally, but when I rotate the phone to landscape with autorotate, the entire app UI looks crazy") followed by explicit direction after a scoping discussion: not a rotate-back prompt, not a separate landscape layout - "nothing. Root-caused first: isMobileActive() reads raw window.matchMedia('(max-width: 767px)') - a typical phone in landscape (e.g. 844x390) crosses that breakpoint and silently flips the whole rendering profile from mobile to DESKTOP cssVars, explaining the original report exactly (a correct portrait paint, then the async settings fetch/resize handler re-applies with the wrong profile). Implemented as 2 dependent parts, gated on real touchscreens only ((hover:none) and (pointer:coarse) and (orientation:landscape), so an ordinary wide desktop browser window already at that aspect ratio is never mistaken for a rotated phone - orientation:landscape alone can't tell those apart): (1) ORIENTATION - .game-container gets position:fixed, rotated 90deg, with its pre-rotation box swapped to 100vh wide x 100vw tall, so its on-screen footprint exactly refits the real landscape screen with no letterboxing gap; (2) SCALE - rotation alone isn't sufficient, since native vw/vh units always measure the TRUE viewport regardless of any transformed ancestor, so everything would still size against the real (landscape) screen, just now also rotated (confirmed by testing part 1 in isolation before adding part 2 - visibly correct orientation, still-oversized content, exactly as predicted). Deliberately scoped to .game-container only - the dev panel is a sibling outside this box and stays completely unaffected (its own drag/resize/clamp math reads real screen coordinates and would break if nested inside a rotated ancestor); 3 dev-only debug overlays (click counter, tap diagnostic panel, round breakdown panel) are DOM descendants of .game-container so they rotate along with everything else, but were deliberately left on plain vw/vh rather than converted to cqw/cqh - dev tooling, out of scope for a player-facing fix, so their own landscape sizing may still look off (acceptable, gated to localhost/dev=1 same as the rest of the dev panel). 2026-09-06 | Added a 'loadingdone' listener alongside document.fonts.ready for the font-content-ratio fix - closes a real race the .ready-only version could lose | Direct follow-up after the font-content-ratio fix's own report: "TEXT ALIGNMEN STILL NOT WORKING," with a 3rd new recording (22s, frame-by-frame confirmed via the established OpenCV method: Target touching the top edge at 220px width, gradually shifting down as the browser widened, a real ~64px swing). Investigated whether Target and Ms/Click might actually be rendering with DIFFERENT fonts (would break the shared-ratio assumption entirely) before assuming a timing issue - checked getComputedStyle().fontFamily on both live, confirmed identical ("Micro 5", monospace) - ruled out. Root-caused instead to a documented, plausible race in document.fonts.ready itself: it's a snapshot of whatever's already in document.fonts AT THE MOMENT it's first accessed, and this project's script runs early in page load, plausibly before the external Google Fonts <link> (in <head>) has even registered "Micro 5" as a pending FontFace - in which case .ready can resolve against an effectively-empty/wrong set and never fire again once Micro 5 actually starts (and later finishes) loading, silently leaving --extrusion-font-content-ratio stuck at whatever the fallback font's ratio was. This session's own testing tool never surfaced the race because its browser profile already had Micro 5 HTTP-cached from dozens of earlier navigations this session, so .ready always happened to resolve correctly there - a genuine environment-parity gap, not a flaw in the fix's own logic (confirmed separately via a live mismatch test in the immediately preceding round, where the formula's predictions matched measurements almost exactly). Added a 'loadingdone' event listener (fires every time a FontFaceSet loading operation completes, including one that only became pending after .ready's own initial snapshot) calling the SAME idempotent re-measure function already used by .ready - safe to fire more than once, always converges to the correct ratio for whichever font is genuinely active. Verified div-tag balance (488/488) and a Function()-based syntax scan before push; confirmed live that the ratio still correctly settles to 1 (Micro 5's own real ratio) in this session's own (already-warm) test tab, with no console errors. Could NOT reproduce the actual race condition itself in this environment (the same font-caching limitation noted above) - gave the user a direct, fast, one-line console diagnostic (read --extrusion-font-content-ratio's live computed value on a fresh hard-reload) to confirm from their own device whether this closes the gap, rather than requiring another full video round-trip regardless of outcome.

2026-09-06 | Found and fixed the REAL cause of the Target/Ms-per-click drift: the pure-CSS calc() fix's own hardcoded "1" assumed every font's content-height equals exactly 1.0x its font-size - wrong for this project's `display=swap` fallback font | Direct pushback after the calc() fix's own "confirmed working" report: "the text alignment is still broken. wtf is the issue an this is a simple fix," with 2 new screen recordings - one showing Target and Ms/Click both at Y offset 14 clearly NOT aligned, the other showing a genuine CROSSOVER (Target's top starts above Ms/Click's, but as the browser widens Ms/Click's top ends up above Target's). Quantitatively confirmed via the established OpenCV frame-by-frame pixel-measurement method: a real, monotonic 66px swing (diff between the two texts' top edges went from +53px to -13px as width increased), ruling out measurement noise. Directly reproduced the mechanism rather than re-theorizing: this project's Google Fonts `<link>` uses `display=swap`, meaning every page load renders a FALLBACK font first while "Micro 5" downloads - measured the fallback's real content-height ratio (glyph height at line-height:1, as a fraction of font-size) at ~1.17, vs Micro 5's own exactly-1.0 (both measured live, not assumed). The prior round's calc() formula hardcoded "1" as this ratio - correct for Micro 5 specifically, but silently wrong for however long the fallback is actually showing, and since the leftover error is a % of a box height that scales with font-size (Scale-With-Browser), this produces a smooth, resize-proportional drift instead of a one-time jump - matching the reported symptom exactly, and explaining why the fix appeared to work in this session's own testing (an already-warm tab where Micro 5 was already cached from prior navigations) but not on the user's fresh page loads. Replaced the hardcoded "1" with --extrusion-font-content-ratio, measured via a new measureFontContentRatio() helper against whichever font is ACTUALLY active - set once immediately (correct even pre-load, matching the fallback's own real ratio) and re-measured exactly once more when document.fonts.ready resolves (a single, non-recursive hook, same safe shape as this project's own earlier font-ready fix). Every element's --anchor-ty calc() reads this one root-level custom property by name, so the single update repaints everything automatically with no per-element re-trigger and no resize-time JS at all - preserving the entire point of the pure-CSS approach; only the FONT-LOAD trigger (a single reliable browser-native promise) needed JS, not resize. Verified live on production via a targeted mismatch test: forced --extrusion-font-content-ratio to the measured fallback value (1.17) while Micro 5 was still the ACTUAL rendering font, at Target's real offset (-15.85) and Ms's (14) - confirmed the resulting position errors matched the formula's own mathematical prediction almost exactly (target: predicted 24.09px error at width 600, measured 24.08px; ms: predicted 7.58px, measured 7.17px) - both confirming the fix's math is correct AND confirming the original bug's mechanism (a ratio mismatch scaling with font-size). Honest caveat given to the user: while the real font is still downloading (a brief, connection-dependent window on a fresh/uncached load), the position now correctly matches whatever the ACTUAL fallback font's metrics are rather than an arbitrary wrong assumption - but it's a bounded, self-correcting window now, not the previous unbounded resize-accumulating drift. 2026-09-06 | Replaced the entire "detect drift after layout, then nudge --anchor-ty" line-height-leading fix with a pure CSS calc() formula - no JS re-trigger of any kind required | Direct follow-up to the ResizeObserver round below: user reported "wasnt fixed" with a 4th screen recording, still showing the same drift via the same frame-by-frame OpenCV pixel measurement used throughout this investigation. Asked directly why matching top-valign + Scale-With-Browser + equal Y-offset numbers didn't guarantee equal top-edge distance at every width - answered honestly: the BOXES were always correctly static (proven, unchanged this round), but line-height (a 3rd factor those settings don't touch) makes the GLYPH drift within its own box, and Target (line-height 1.2) and Ms-per-click (line-height 0.65, deliberately different for its 3-line compact layout) drift in opposite directions as Scale-With-Browser grows font-size. User's own direct follow-up ("so cant you just subtract the top padding that is caused by line height?") confirmed the exact direction already underway: replaced correctValignLeading() (a runtime measurement of the real glyph-vs-box gap, re-run via 3 stacked trigger mechanisms - a debounced resize listener, a ResizeObserver, and a document.fonts.ready hook - none of which passed on the user's real Chrome DevTools Device Toolbar despite passing every test this environment could construct) with a closed-form formula: the leading above an n-line, line-height-LH block's first glyph, as a fraction of the block's own total height, is (1-LH)/(2*n*LH) - algebraically font-size-independent (F cancels top and bottom), so it needs computing exactly ONCE per element from 2 already-known constants (its line-height cssVar, already user-adjustable via each element's own "Line Spacing" slider, and its line count, a fixed structural fact of a static text template - Ms-per-click is always exactly 3 lines by design, not something that varies at runtime). Expressed directly as a CSS calc() string assigned to --anchor-ty (e.g. `calc((1 - var(--target-line-height, 1)) / (2 * 1 * var(--target-line-height, 1)) * 100%)`) instead of a JS-computed number, so the browser's own layout engine re-evaluates it automatically on every single paint - eliminating the entire class of "did the right event fire at the right time" bugs that caused all 3 prior rounds to fail silently on the user's real device. This is the generalized version of a formula-only approach tried earlier THIS SAME investigation that fixed Target (1 line) but left Ms-per-click drifting - that attempt only used (1-LH)/(2*LH), missing the line-count term; adding line count (VALIGN_LINE_COUNT, currently just {'--ms-per-click': 3}, default 1) is what makes it correct for both now. Removed correctValignLeading() and all 3 of its trigger mechanisms entirely (the debounced resize-settle listener, the valignResizeObserver and its setup, the document.fonts.ready hook) as fully superseded dead code - none of them do anything the new calc() doesn't already handle natively. User also separately asked for a Target Text line-spacing slider "maybe if i set that as the same as ms/click this will all be fixed" - checked first rather than assuming: a "Line Spacing" slider (--target-line-height) already exists for Target on both Desktop and Mobile tabs (added in an earlier round, default 1.2), so no new control was needed; reported this back rather than adding a duplicate, and noted that matching the VALUE alone wouldn't have fully fixed the alignment on its own (any line-height != 1 still leaves SOME leading gap unless it's exactly 1.0) - the calc() fix in this entry corrects for whatever line-height value is set, matched or not. Verified live (no manual resize-event dispatch, proving the fix needs none): read each element's raw inline --anchor-ty style and confirmed it holds the exact calc() string with the correct line-count baked in (1 for Target, 3 for Ms-per-click); measured the real glyph-vs-box gap ratio (Range.getBoundingClientRect() vs the element's own getBoundingClientRect(), the same method used to root-cause this bug originally) at viewport width 375 (Target: 0.0800, Ms-per-click: -0.0921) and again at width 1280 with zero JS re-trigger in between (Target: 0.0827, Ms-per-click: -0.0917) - both ratios held constant across a 375->1280px resize, matching the formula's own font-size-independence claim algebraically as well as empirically. Verified div-tag balance (488/488) and a Function()-based syntax scan of the inline script block before push. 2026-09-06 | Fixed the REAL cause of Target/Ms-per-click drifting on resize: correctValignLeading() was additive, not idempotent | Follow-up bug report after the earlier same-day "top edge alignment" fix: "same issue. after hard refresh, in mobile browser, when i resize the browser, the target text and the ms/click text, their offset from top edge doesnt scale the same" then "the larger the browser, the further down the target text shifts down." Extensive live re-testing (up to 700px width, height changes, a fresh uncached tab) could not reproduce ANY drift using the earlier fix - a real, misleading dead end that delayed finding the actual cause. First follow-up theory: webfonts not yet loaded when the correction first runs, so it measures the FALLBACK font's leading (confirmed genuinely different via direct measurement: 17px real "Micro 5" font vs 2px generic monospace at the same size) - implemented a document.fonts.ready re-run, but testing it via a mocked fonts object exposed a DIFFERENT bug: the re-run was self-recursive (correctValignLeading() rescheduling itself on every call while status wasn't 'loaded'), which cascaded into repeated corrections and corrupted --anchor-ty to a nonsensical -909044%/-781684% in 2 separate test runs - replaced with a single non-recursive top-level hook. Even with that fixed, a careful re-verification (checking whether the GLYPH's absolute position matched a cleanly-computed baseline, not just whether the internal glyph-to-box gap shrank - which, on reflection, it structurally never can: a transform:translate() shifts the box AND everything inside it, including the glyph, by the same amount, so it can never change that internal, line-height-driven relationship) surfaced the ACTUAL root cause: the function computed each correction as (currentTyPercent - newCorrection), additive on top of whatever --anchor-ty already held. Since the internal gap it measures never shrinks toward zero (it's invariant to anchor-ty), EVERY re-run added another FULL correction on top of the last. window.addEventListener('resize', applyActiveVars) already calls this on every single resize event - a real drag-resize (or a mobile device's own resize handling) fires many events in quick succession, each one stacking another correction. This is almost certainly the exact, precise mechanism behind the report: a bigger resize gesture directly means more resize events, which directly means more accumulated corrections - unrelated to font-loading timing, which was a real but secondary, much smaller contributor. Fixed by computing each correction as an ABSOLUTE offset from the base valign value (0% for "top") rather than an additive delta - idempotent now, safe to call any number of times from any call site. Verified live: fired 30 resize events in a row on a single viewport (the exact scenario that would have compounded under the old bug) and confirmed the glyph position was byte-identical before and after; separately simulated a full drag-resize gesture (a 15-step width sweep 375->700->375px, 2 resize events fired per step, 30 total) and confirmed both Target's and Ms-per-click's glyph positions landed at their exact expected stable values throughout. Verified div-tag balance and a Function()-based syntax scan of every inline script block before each of the 4 pushes in this round.

2026-09-06 | Fixed the REAL root cause of persistent "the animation on desktop is still very laggy" reports - a per-tap performance bug in the click-burst effect, unrelated to any of this session's earlier CSS-transition-timing work | Per direct report, investigated by MEASURING rather than re-checking the already-confirmed-instant press/release transition settings (desktop/mobile press-ms and release-ms were both still correctly 0/0 - this was never a regression of that earlier fix). Timed a real dispatched tap directly (performance.now() around dispatchEvent): the pointerdown handler's own synchronous execution took 31-44ms - more than 2 full frame budgets (16.7ms each at 60fps) of main-thread blocking on EVERY tap, independent of and unrelated to any CSS transition duration. Isolated the cost by timing spawnClickBurst() and setShadowButtonPressed() separately: setShadowButtonPressed() was ~0ms (not the cause, despite being this session's earlier prime suspect for per-tap cost); spawnClickBurst() alone was 35-65ms - the actual dominant cost, and specifically sampleClickRoutePiecesInAngleRange()'s call to the SVG path's own getPointAtLength(), 201 times per side x 2 sides = 402 expensive geometry calls on every single tap. Confirmed via grep that clickRouteLeftPath/clickRouteRightPath's `d` attribute is set exactly once at page load and never reassigned - the path's raw geometry never changes at runtime, only the floorDeg/ceilingDeg filter (dev-panel sliders) can vary between calls, and that filter only touches the OUTPUT of the scan, never the expensive per-point sampling itself. Fixed by caching the raw 201-point scan per path element (computed once, lazily, on first use, in a Map keyed by the path element) - every call after the very first now does only cheap array filtering against the cached data instead of re-walking the path's geometry from scratch; zero behavior change, since the cached scan data is byte-for-byte identical to what a fresh scan would produce (the path never moves). Verified live via direct before/after timing: spawnClickBurst() dropped from 35-65ms every call to a one-time ~47ms first call (populating the cache) followed by 0-1.5ms on every subsequent call - confirmed via a real 10-tap dispatch loop through the actual gameButton pointerdown handler that end-to-end per-tap synchronous work dropped from 31-44ms to 0.7-2.2ms, a ~20-40x improvement, now comfortably within a single frame budget. Confirmed no console errors and the click-burst effect's own visual output is unaffected (same qualifying-point selection logic, just reading from a cache instead of the DOM). Verified extracted-script syntax.

This bug affected BOTH group reordering and individual-setting reordering (one shared function) and had presumably been broken since the feature was first built - a prior session's own full CLAUDE.md Section 12 audit had marked this feature "implemented" without live-testing the actual drag outcome, only that the setup call existed. Fixed by negating both conditions; live-verified both group-level (Dev Panel/Main Button swapped) and setting-level (Diameter row moved) reordering now genuinely reorder the DOM. (4) COLLAPSED PANEL TOO TALL, per direct report with a screenshot/reference line showing the intended bottom edge - root cause: the base .dev-panel rule's own min-height:120px (sized for the EXPANDED state's usability) was never overridden by .dev-panel.panel-collapsed, so the floor still applied even though the collapsed state's only visible content (the header, ~36px) needs far less. Added min-height:0 to the collapsed-state rule; live-verified collapsed height dropped from 120px to 76px (the remaining 25px is the header's own pre-existing margin-bottom + the panel's own padding, not leftover empty space). (5) Investigated "i cant drag the dev panel downwards. i can make it shorter, just not click and drag" extensively - synthetic mouse AND touch pointer-event drags in both collapsed and expanded panel states, multiple distances, all moved and clamped exactly as expected; could not reproduce any downward-drag-specific failure. No code-level bug found in this specific mechanism (distinct from the unrelated reorder bug above) - flagged honestly rather than guessing a blind fix; the user should retest and report more specifics (browser, exact repro steps) if it persists. (6) ROTATING "?" PERFORMANCE, per direct report ("the rotating question mark animation slows down the project a lot. when it appears everything is laggier") - root-caused: tickTryAgainRotate() rebuilds the extrusion layer's entire text-shadow from scratch and reapplies it every single animation frame (up to 60/sec) - at this element's typical depth/thickness (23/24), buildCombinedShadowXComp() generates (23+1)*8 border-ring layers + 23 extrusion layers = ~215 individual text-shadow layers, and a multi-hundred-layer text-shadow is one of the most expensive things a browser can repaint. Throttled that specific rebuild to a minimum 50ms (~20fps) interval via a new tryAgainRotateShadowLastUpdate timestamp, while the glyph/extrusion scaleX transforms (cheap, GPU-composited, and what actually needs to look smooth) stay at full per-frame rate - verified via a simulated 60-frame/1-second run (mocked Date.now(), no real-time wait needed) that the expensive rebuild now fires 20 times instead of 60, a genuine 3x reduction in the dominant per-frame cost, with no change to the visible rotation smoothness at this feature's normal (multi-second) rotation speeds. Verified div tag balance (392/392), `node --check`, and settings-log JSON validity before push.

2026-09-03 | Live-verified the reconciled win-trigger fix (follow-up to the 2 entries directly below) | Real elapsed-wall-clock tests on the deployed site (nested setTimeout chains, matching this session's established working pattern) gave confusing/contradictory results here - traced to the Browser pane tab being throttled as backgrounded, which Chrome clamps to roughly one setTimeout tick per second regardless of the delay requested, badly distorting anything timed in the 100-500ms range and occasionally reordering which of 2 near-simultaneous timers fires first. 2026-09-03 | Correction to the entry directly below: the "wins immediately" fix didn't actually take effect, due to an unnoticed conflict with an earlier fix from a concurrent session | The prior entry's own claim of live-verifying all 3 branches was written before that verification actually ran, and turned out to be wrong once it did - a real process mistake, not just a documentation gap; flagging it plainly rather than quietly fixing the code and leaving the false claim standing. That was a real bug and a reasonable fix for it in isolation. My own fix (the entry below) took a different, incompatible approach - correcting armPerClickTimeout()'s fire handler itself to check for win-at-target - without noticing the OTHER session had already worked around the same root problem by preventing the timer from arming at target at all. 2026-09-03 | Reaching exact target now wins immediately once the per-click window passes, instead of waiting for the round to time out | Per direct bug report describing the exact intended sequence: reach target -> the per-click ceiling timer (added earlier this session) counts down -> if it elapses with no further tap, win immediately. Root cause: armPerClickTimeout()'s own timer (added to make the LOSE side of the per-click ceiling fire from silence alone, not just reactively on a late tap) always ended the round as a loss ('too-slow') when it fired, with no check for the case where the player was actually sitting exactly at target - so hitting the goal and correctly stopping still silently waited out the much longer aggregate round countdown (maxTimeMs*targetCount) before resolving, even though the per-click timer (armed fresh on every tap, including the target-reaching one) was already ticking down to the same conclusion far sooner. Fixed by checking gameState.currentTapCount === gameState.targetCount when that timer fires: win immediately if so, keep the existing 'too-slow' loss otherwise. Overshoot is unaffected (still an instant loss on the tap itself, checked earlier in handleGameButtonPress() and unreachable by this timer since the round would have already ended). 2026-09-03 | Restored touch drag-to-reorder via a hold-to-arm gate, on top of the mobile-scroll fix | Per direct follow-up ("well you can do dragging if i click and hold for more than a second or something" / "versus scrolling which i would start scrolling faster than a second") - the earlier mobile-scroll fix (removing touch-action:none from the drag handles) also incidentally broke touch-based reordering, since both are the same vertical gesture on the same element with no CSS-only way to tell them apart. Fixed properly: setupDragReorder()'s pointerdown, for pointerType 'touch' specifically, no longer arms a drag immediately - it starts a 1000ms hold timer instead. If the touch moves more than 10px before that timer fires, the hold is cancelled and nothing is prevented, so the gesture reaches the browser's native scroll exactly as if this code didn't exist. If held still past 1000ms, THEN it commits to drag mode (arms `dragging`, marks `moved` true immediately since the hold itself already established intent) - only from that point does pointermove call preventDefault() and reorder, which is reliable precisely because no motion occurred during the hold, so the browser hadn't started its own scroll yet to compete with. Mouse/pen are completely unaffected - immediate, movement-threshold-based dragging exactly as before, since touch-action never governed them. Added pointercancel handling (a pre-existing gap, now load-bearing - a stray hold-timer left running past a cancelled gesture could mis-arm a later touch) and made the pointermove listener explicitly {passive:false} so preventDefault() is guaranteed to take effect once armed. Verified div tag balance (390/390) and `node --check`.

2026-09-03 | Hardened dev-panel drag/resize against mobile touch drags cutting out early | Per direct report ("resizing and dragging is still very hit or miss on mobile... drag the top edge up. The edge may move up maybe 5-10 px then it stops. then i have to do that repeatedly"). Both the resize handles (setupPanelResizeHandle, all 8 edges/corners) and the header drag (.dev-header) already had touch-action:none and setPointerCapture() from an earlier fix, so this isn't a repeat of that bug - static analysis without a real touchscreen to reproduce on couldn't pin down one single certain cause, so applied 3 standard, low-risk hardening measures together rather than guessing at just one: (1) added explicit e.preventDefault() on pointerdown for both the resize handles and the header drag, on top of the existing touch-action:none, since that CSS property alone doesn't guarantee every mobile browser suppresses ALL of its own default touch handling the instant a touch starts; (2) widened the 4 edge strips from 12px to 20px (matching the corner handles, which weren't reported as broken) - a real fingertip's contact area is well above the ~44px minimum both major mobile platforms recommend, so a 12px strip was an easy miss even when aiming correctly; (3) added overscroll-behavior:none to html/body to suppress the browser's own pull-to-refresh/rubber-band gesture, which operates above the DOM at the browser-chrome level and can still compete for a touch sequence even when the touched element itself has touch-action:none - directly relevant given the report's own repro was dragging the TOP edge, exactly where that gesture engages. 2026-09-02 | Win-detection fix, take 2: the previous fix (2 commits ago) wasn't actually sufficient | Live-tested the win-bug fix by instrumenting endGame() to log its own (won, reason, timestamp) arguments and running a real round through the actual game functions with real (tool-level, not in-page setTimeout - see below) delays between taps. Result: still lost, with endGame(false, "too-slow") firing ~2s after the target-reaching tap. Root cause of the incomplete fix: skipping the RE-ARM on the target-reaching tap wasn't enough, because the PREVIOUS tap (target-1) had already armed a perClickTimeoutId that was still pending - nothing was cancelling it, so it fired on its own regardless of the later tap succeeding. Fixed by explicitly clearTimeout()-ing and nulling gameState.perClickTimeoutId once target is reached, not just declining to renew it. Verified div tag balance (411/411) and `node --check`; live re-test pending this deploy (see next entry once confirmed). Also notable from this debugging session, for future reference: (1) real end-to-end testing of this timing-sensitive logic via separate tool round-trips is unreliable - the page keeps running in real wall-clock time regardless of the agent's own processing latency between calls, producing multi-SECOND gaps between what were meant to be ~500ms-spaced taps; a single script execution with internal `await new Promise(r=>setTimeout(...))` calls is the only reliable way to control real elapsed time precisely; (2) even within one script, a backgrounded/non-composited browser tab clamps every setTimeout to a minimum of roughly 1000ms per call regardless of the requested duration, and a LOOP of many short waits compounds this far worse than one single longer wait - budget test timing accordingly (larger maxTimeMs margins, fewer/longer waits over many short polls).

2026-09-02 | Fixed a severe win-detection bug (reaching target almost never won); added the "?" glyph's own extrusion/border sliders; added actual click count to Round Breakdown | Three items. (1) WIN BUG, per direct report ("I click the correct times but it doesnt say i win"): armPerClickTimeout() re-armed a maxTimeMs "no more taps = lose" timer on EVERY accepted tap, including the one that reached exactly targetCount - but the round is only supposed to resolve (win or lose) when the round's own countdown expires at maxTimeMs*targetCount from the FIRST tap. Since the last tap's re-armed timer fires at (that tap's time + maxTimeMs), and a successful run's last tap virtually always lands well before (targetCount-1)*maxTimeMs into the round, that per-click timer fires BEFORE the round countdown for any targetCount > 1 - falsely declaring "too-slow" and cancelling the round countdown before it could ever check the win condition. This wasn't a rare race, it was the near-universal outcome. Fixed by not re-arming that timer once currentTapCount reaches targetCount - there's no further tap being waited for at that point, the round now correctly waits out its own countdown and wins. (2) Per follow-up request, the "?" now has its own independent Extrusion Depth/Overall Border Thickness/Overall Border Color (new extrusionVars.questionMarkDepth/questionMarkOverallBorderThickness/questionMarkOverallBorderColor + mobile twins, new --try-again-question-mark-extrusion-shadow cssVar), on top of the Font Size/Color/X/Y from 2 commits ago - questionMarkFillColor doubles as the extrusion's own trail color, matching the one-picker-drives-both convention every other element uses. New sliders in both the Desktop and Mobile Rotation Animation sections. (3) Round Breakdown now shows actual "Clicks: N" alongside the existing "Click Count Target" - new roundHistory[].clicks field (currentRoundTaps.length), captured per round in endGame(). Verified div tag balance (411/411) and `node --check`. The win-bug fix's math was traced through by hand (concrete example: targetCount=5, maxTimeMs=1000 - old code, per-click timer fires at t=3000 vs round countdown at t=5000, false loss; confirmed the fix removes that premature timer) rather than live-tested end-to-end yet, since a real-time round takes several real seconds to play out - flagging that a live full-round test is still worth doing before considering this fully confirmed.

2026-09-02 | Fixed rotation animation defaulting to the wrong extrusion mode - was causing a solid black blob at the compressed rotation angles | Per a screen recording showing "the rotation doesn't look right" - extracted and reviewed frames (this environment has no ffmpeg but does have Python's cv2, used that instead). Root cause: checkboxTryAgainRotateExtrusionMatchesGlyph shipped with NO `checked` attribute and its cssVars default was `--try-again-rotate-extrusion-matches-glyph: 0` (independent mode) - directly contradicting that feature's own changelog entry 2 commits ago, which claimed it "ships checked by default, reproducing the old single-unit look." In independent mode the extrusion follows sin(angle) while the glyph follows cos(angle) - opposite curves - so right when the glyph compresses to a thin sliver (edge-on), the extrusion is at ITS OWN maximum width, casting a full-width shadow-stack "?" behind the squished glyph; visually this reads as a solid black blob, not a clean 3D rotation, exactly matching the recording. Fixed both the checkbox (added `checked`) and the cssVars default (0->1). Also found and fixed the same stale `0` sitting in the git-tracked settings log itself (data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json, from an earlier real Save) - a code-level default fix alone wouldn't have been visible, since a stored setting overrides the default on load; patched just that one field, left every other saved value (a real, currently-tuned settings dump - button diameter, colors, positions, etc.) untouched. 2026-09-02 | Backend save/load fully working end to end; LOAD now fetches live from GitHub's API instead of the stale static file | Debugged a persistent live report of SAVE showing "Server not configured"/"Unauthorized"/GitHub 403 across several rounds - root causes were, in order: (1) env vars hadn't actually been redeployed yet after being added; (2) the GitHub token's Contents permission genuinely wasn't Read+write despite appearing correct, and separately a fine-grained token kept hitting GitHub's known rough edges with the Contents "create or update file" endpoint - switched to a classic PAT with the `repo` scope (matching what this environment's own `gh` CLI already uses successfully against this exact repo) and that resolved the 403; (3) DEV_PANEL_SAVE_SECRET had drifted out of sync with the client's copy at some point during the GITHUB_TOKEN troubleshooting - re-set to match. Verified with a real connectivity POST that got a genuine 200 + commit sha back. That test incidentally exposed a real architecture gap: data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json is served to loadSettings()/acquireSavedSettings() as a same-origin static file, which only reflects whatever was live at the LAST deployment, not the latest git commit - confirmed directly (the test commit landed in git immediately, but the live site kept serving the pre-save value until a later, unrelated deploy went out). Fixed per explicit choice (offered two options, this one picked): acquireSavedSettings()'s non-file: branch now fetches straight from GitHub's Contents API (public repo, no auth needed for reads, base64-decoded client-side) instead of the static path - always reflects the true latest commit, no redeploy needed to see a save reflected on load. Accepted tradeoff: this counts against GitHub's unauthenticated rate limit (60 req/hr/IP); a rate-limited or otherwise failed fetch already falls back to defaults gracefully, same as any other unreachable-source case. Also reverted an accidental {"test":true} write to the settings log (from the connectivity-test POST) back to {} via a normal commit, not through the secret-gated endpoint. Verified div tag balance (393/393), `node --check`, and the base64-decode round-trip logic in isolation (Node Buffer equivalent of atob/decodeURIComponent/escape, including GitHub's actual embedded-newline base64 format); live-verified the full SAVE→commit→(new deploy)→LOAD path via curl and the GitHub API.

2026-09-02 | SAVE is now git-only - dropped the localStorage write entirely, LOAD/RESET now pull from the git-tracked settings log instead | Per direct follow-up correction: the just-shipped settings-sync (previous entry) kept localStorage as a fallback alongside the new git write, matching CLAUDE.md 12l's literal "not just local, but also git" wording - explicitly overridden for this project: SAVE should be git-only. saveSettings() no longer writes localStorage or shows the old "Settings saved!" alert; it only POSTs to /api/save-settings, with the existing status label now reading "NOT saved: ..." on failure (git sync failing now means nothing persisted at all, not just a missed extra copy, so that has to be unambiguous). loadSettings() is now async - fetches data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json directly (same-origin static file, no secret needed for a read) instead of reading localStorage; resetDevSettings() (RESET button + R key) is unaffected since it already just calls loadSettings(). Fixed by splitting init in two: all event-listener wiring plus one paint pass using hardcoded defaults runs synchronously first (so the page never sits waiting on a network round-trip just to become visible/interactive), then a second async block awaits loadSettings() and re-applies on top once it resolves. The round-breakdown-panel/dev-panel position-sentinel resolution (previously inline, sentinel-vs-real-value logic) was extracted into resolvePositionSentinels() so it could safely run in both passes - the first pass keeps a first-time visitor from ever seeing a literal unresolved sentinel value (e.g. dev panel at left:-1px) before the fetch lands, the second correctly no-ops instead of clobbering whatever the fetch actually restored. Accepted, documented trade-off: on a slow connection there can now be a brief visible moment of default positions/colors before a saved setup applies, since there's no local copy left to paint instantly from. 2026-09-02 | Fixed the rotating "?"'s extrusion/border reading as too thick past ~45deg | Per direct follow-up report ("when the glyph is rotate maybe more than 45 degrees, the extrusion starts lookin too thick. it should be a constant thickness"). Root cause: CSS transform scales an element's text-shadow along with its glyph, but scaleX() only affects the X axis - the extrusion layer's text-shadow (buildCombinedShadow(), fixed-pixel diagonal depth offsets + an 8-direction border ring) was being built ONCE (static, unscaled) and then squished non-uniformly by the SAME scaleX() applied to foreshorten the glyph: horizontal shadow offsets shrank toward zero while vertical offsets stayed full-size, steepening the diagonal depth trail and making the border ring's horizontal reach collapse while its vertical reach didn't - both read as "thicker" as the angle increased toward edge-on. Fixed with new X-compensated shadow builders (buildExtrusionShadowXComp/buildBorderRingShadowXComp/buildCombinedShadowXComp) that pre-divide every X offset by the same scaleX about to be applied, cancelling the squish exactly - only used for this one rotating layer, since the other 5 extruded text elements are never scaleX-transformed and keep using the plain static builders. Recomputed every animation frame in tickTryAgainRotate() (can no longer be a static CSS var, since the compensation factor changes continuously with the rotation angle) and applied as an inline text-shadow directly on the extrusion element, with a small hard floor (0.05) on the compensation divisor to avoid a divide-by-zero blowup at the exact edge-on instant when Minimum Width is set to 0. Verified div tag balance (393/393) and `node --check` before push.

2026-09-02 | Bundled fix: per-click ceiling now fires from silence alone (not just on a late tap), and the rotating "?" no longer shows a second visible glyph | Per 2 direct follow-up reports on the same-session work above. (1) LOSE TRIGGER: the previous fix only checked the gap REACTIVELY when a new tap arrived - it could never catch "the player simply stops tapping and never comes back", since there was no later event to react to ("if i dont click again for a duration longer than the Ms/Click number I dont lose... the lose screen should trigger the moment the time has passed"). (2) ROTATION GLYPH: found the actual root cause of "the rotating question mark still shows as 2 question marks" - #startButtonFlashCharExtrusion (the 3D-depth layer) had its own fully opaque, independently-visible glyph fill (color: var(--try-again-fill-color)), not just its text-shadow. 2026-09-02 | Text Edit Mode: drag a bounding box's left/right edges to set that text element's wrap width | Per explicit request ("I want to be able to resize text boxes in text edit mode... control when the text goes to the next line"). Added a 10px drag handle at each edge of every bounding box (pointer-events:auto, unlike the box itself) - dragging sets a per-element max-width (new textEditWrapWidths, null by default = today's unconstrained shrink-to-fit behavior, unchanged). white-space:pre-line (added earlier for Shift+Enter) already wraps at a constrained width on its own, so this needed no white-space change - just the max-width. Anchor-aware: reads each element's current text-align anchor (align-left/align-right/center, from applyTextAlignAnchors()) so the DRAGGED edge is the one that visually moves - the anchor's own fixed edge can't be dragged (its handle no-ops, since that edge is pinned by the anchor's own CSS transform) and center-anchored text grows symmetrically (both edges move together, so the dragged edge tracks the pointer at double speed to compensate - center anchor is otherwise the box growing from its middle, which would make the dragged edge lag at half the pointer's actual movement). Persisted via textEditWrapWidths in copySettings()/saveSettings()/loadSettings(), same pattern as textOverrides. Verified div tag balance (373/373) and `node --check` before push. Known scope limit, not a defect: the anchor-aware speed compensation is exact for left/right-anchored text and center anchor's symmetric case, but doesn't attempt to keep the OPPOSITE (non-dragged) edge perfectly pixel-frozen in every case - flagged here rather than silently claimed as pixel-perfect.

2026-09-02 | Rotation Animation: extrusion/border can now scale independently from the glyph | Per explicit request/discussion. Previously the "?" 's extrusion/border was baked into the same element as the glyph, so scaleX() transformed both as one unit (a single element's text-shadow always moves with it). 2026-09-02 | Hide Shadow Frame Index A/B now applies per-side and takes a free-text position/range list | Per explicit request. Previously A and B were two typed CUTOFFS both applied to BOTH click-burst sides identically (a single number, hiding a range from one edge). Now: A applies to the LEFT side only, B to the RIGHT only - relabeled "Hide Shadow (Left) - Frame Positions:" / "Hide Shadow (Right) - Frame Positions:". The input changed from a numeric cutoff to free text supporting a comma-separated list of positions and/or ranges, e.g. "0=>1, 3, -4=>-7" - position 0 is the topmost piece in that side's own top-to-bottom order, a negative position counts from the bottom instead (-1 = bottommost), and "a=>b" is an inclusive range in either order. New parseHideShadowSpec()/resolveHideShadowPosition() replace the old resolveHideShadowRange(); out-of-range positions are silently dropped rather than erroring, same spirit as the old cutoff's clamping. New .dev-text-input-wide CSS class (160px, left-aligned) since a position list needs real room, unlike the short numeric fields elsewhere in this section. Defaults changed from 3/-2 (cutoffs applied to both sides) to "0=>2" (left) / "-2=>-1" (right) (positions applied per-side) - the closest equivalent recreation of the old default look, though the underlying behavior is genuinely different now (per-side instead of shared). Verified div tag balance (363/363) and `node --check` before push; live-verified the parser directly - the exact example spec from the request ("0=>1, 3, -4=>-7" against 10 pieces) resolved to exactly {0,1,3,4,5,6} as hand-computed, confirmed out-of-range terms are silently dropped, confirmed an empty spec hides nothing, and confirmed a reversed range ("-7=>-4" vs "-4=>-7") produces the identical result.

2026-09-02 | Fixed: rapid clicking always won, regardless of timing | Per explicit bug report ("the winning losing is bugging. I win if i just keep clicking"). Root cause: the countdown-timer redesign made reaching exact target count win the round IMMEDIATELY on that tap - since currentTapCount increments by exactly 1 per accepted tap, mashing the button as fast as possible is guaranteed to pass through the exact target value at some point, winning instantly with no real timing pressure (this was the exact consequence flagged, but not yet reported as a problem, when that redesign shipped). Fixed by removing the immediate-win branch from handleGameButtonPress() - reaching exact target now just stops the round from being winnable further (an extra tap still instantly loses via the existing overshoot check) without ending it. The round now only resolves in startRoundCountdown()'s own timeout callback, when the total time budget naturally runs out - win if still at exactly targetCount at that moment, undershoot-loss otherwise. This restores the real timing pressure the countdown was originally meant to represent. Verified div tag balance (294/294) and `node --check` before push; live-verified all 3 paths via direct function calls and setTimeout interception (to avoid this session's known background-tab timer-throttling unreliability) - exact-hit-then-wait-for-timeout correctly wins, undershoot-at-timeout correctly loses, and overshoot still loses immediately as before.

Confirmed the root cause numerically (not just visually) via an offscreen-canvas alpha-channel scan of the actual rendered shadow SVGs: 615 (button) and 7721 (base) partial-alpha gap pixels sat between adjacent path edges - real sub-pixel geometric gaps, invisible in the real colored artwork (similar-toned adjacent fills mask them) but exposed once flattened to one flat gray. Live-tested the fix before writing any code: adding a stroke to every path in the shadow's own SVG copies bridges these gaps regardless of blur, since stroke color is irrelevant post-flatten (only width matters). Measured across strokeWidth 0/0.5/1/1.5/2/3 - 2 SVG units (viewBox ~590 wide) took base to 0 gap pixels and button to 9 (99%+ reduction), with negligible outer-edge growth (~0.34% of the shape's own width) applied locally per-path rather than a uniform scale() from a shared center (unlike an earlier-tried base-shadow scale() fix that visibly shifted the shadow - see the z-index comment on .shadow-caster-base-layer). Added `.shadow-caster-layer svg path { stroke: #000; stroke-width: 2; }` - scoped to only the shadow's own SVG copies, the real colored artwork (.base-svg-layer/.game-button) is completely untouched. Deliberately did NOT extend this to the click-burst particle shadow - it uses a single simple sprite, not the multi-path button/base artwork, so there's no evidence it shares this issue and it wasn't part of what was reported. Verified div tag balance (293/293) and `node --check` on the extracted inline script before push.

2026-08-31 | Redesigned the Floor/Ceiling angle inputs: they now filter WHICH PART of the guide curve qualifies as a spawn origin (measured from the button's center), not flight direction | Per explicit clarification: the previous implementation (earlier same-day entry) used the 4 angle inputs purely to assign each particle's flight direction via a deterministic zone-slot, while origins still came from equal-arc-length division of the WHOLE curve regardless of angle.
