A curve stretch entirely outside the requested range is simply never used; if NOTHING on a side's curve qualifies, that side spawns 0 particles that click (not an error). Flight direction now comes directly from each origin's own radial position from center (dirX/dirY, computed during the same scan) plus a small fixed +/-4deg jitter (CLICK_BURST_ANGLE_JITTER_RAD), replacing the old deterministic zone-slot-angle system entirely - removed the now-dead clickBurstUserAngleToTrigRad() call site and the zone/zoneSliceWidth variables (kept the conversion helper's math, added its inverse, clickBurstTrigRadToUserDeg(), for the new angle-scan). 2026-08-31 | Replaced the procedural angle-zone click-burst spawn system with an artist-authored guide-curve system | The user created data/BUTTON/CLICK GUIDE.svg - one open reference path per side, drawn in the SAME viewBox as the button/base artwork (0 0 589.79 605.11), representing the intended click-burst flight route. Per explicit instruction ('divide the curve into 4, and each piece will dictate where a click can generate from. So every click, both side generates 4'): each curve is now divided into 4 equal-arc-length pieces via the browser's own SVGPathElement.getTotalLength()/getPointAtLength() (not a hand-rolled bezier-length approximation - see the shadow-transform fix earlier this session for why hand-derived geometry math specifically was worth avoiding here), sampled at each quarter's midpoint. Every click now spawns exactly 4 particles per side (8 total, both real and shadow), replacing the previous random 2-4-count, angle-zone-based system (RIGHT_ZONE/LEFT_ZONE/SEAM_Y_FRACTION, all removed). Each of the 4 points also carries the curve's own local tangent direction (a small arc-length finite difference), used as that particle's initial outward flight direction, so motion reads as following the authored route rather than an unrelated procedural angle. The 2 guide paths are held as real (invisible, 0x0, overflow:hidden) DOM <path> elements so the browser's native path-length math does the sampling; the left path's small disconnected second subpath (a stray decorative mark, not part of the route) was excluded, using only its first subpath. Origins are mapped from the guide SVG's viewBox space onto the button's own rendered bounding box using the same scale mapping the artwork's own <svg viewBox> performs. 2026-08-31 | Replaced the hand-derived shadow-transform math with the real CSS transform, applied directly (per explicit request) | Diagnosed via ANOTHER new screen recording (extracted with OpenCV): shadows were landing far from the button, effectively 'coming from under the browser's bottom edge'. Measured live: the seam origin's own offset from the button's center (~40px) was coming out of the previous JS reimplementation (transformShadowPoint()) amplified to ~127px - a 3.2x distortion, not the 1:1 correspondence a genuine 'same transform' should have. Root cause: hand-deriving the equivalent point-transform math in JS is not actually equivalent to how a CSS transform matrix operates on a whole subtree - close enough to look plausible in some tests, wrong enough to compound real errors in others (this is now the 3rd distinct bug found in that hand-derived approach). Fixed per direct instruction ('look at the physical transformation we applied to base and button, then apply those exact as they are') by deleting the math entirely: two new containers (.click-burst-shadow-container Right/Left, one per side since the two sides need mirrored skew) each get a CSS `transform` that's a byte-for-byte copy of .shadow-caster-layer's own (translate/rotate/scale/skewX/scaleY, same values, same order), with `transform-origin` set to the button's own center - set once per burst in spawnClickBurst(). Individual shadow particles now use the EXACT SAME left/top/offsetPath as their real counterpart, completely unwarped - the browser's own transform math (applied to the whole container) does the actual warping, identical to how the real button/base shadow works, instead of a from-scratch JS reimplementation of that math. Removed transformShadowPoint() and all the now-dead per-particle skew/stretch/rotate/offset variables entirely. Verified div tag balance (255/255), CSS comment balance (53/53), and `node --check` on the extracted inline script before push.

2026-08-31 | Fixed click-burst shadows rendering as thin slivers pointing the wrong direction (often straight down) | Root-caused via a NEW screen recording the user provided (datalog/Recording 2026-08-31 044555.mp4, extracted with OpenCV, same technique as the earlier '2 frame sets' investigation) rather than guessed from description alone - frames clearly showed thin gray vertical daggers hanging straight down from the shadow, completely unrelated to the fanned-out direction of their real counterparts. Checked the raw CLICK1 artwork directly: it's a downward-pointing triangular shard, not a symmetric shape. The real particle already handles this correctly via offset-rotate:auto 90deg, which continuously reorients the sprite to match its OWN current flight-path tangent. The shadow, from the previous round of fixes, used offset-rotate:0deg (fixed, never adapting) PLUS a separate fixed transform: rotate(41deg) skewX(-31deg) scaleY(1.8) on the sprite itself - a single constant orientation applied to every particle regardless of its own individual flight angle. Across a burst fanning across a 120deg zone per side, that fixed orientation could only ever coincidentally match ONE specific flight direction - for most particles the sprite's rendered orientation had nothing to do with where it was actually traveling, which is exactly what reads as 'pointing down' or generally wrong. Fixed by making the shadow's sprite orientation follow its own path again (removed the offset-rotate:0deg override, inheriting .click-burst-particle's own auto 90deg) and removing the fixed rotate/skewX/scaleY sprite transform entirely (kept only the mirror, exactly matching the real particle's own transform) - the 'cast shadow' warp now comes entirely from the already-correct WARPED PATH (transformShadowPoint(), pivoted at the button's own center, unchanged from the previous fix) plus the gray filter, not from a second, non-adaptive rotation layered on top. This does NOT reintroduce the earlier '2 frames sets' bug (auto-rotate stacking with a fixed transform) since there is no longer a second, independent rotation source at all - the shadow's rotation behavior is now identical to the real particle's. Verified div tag balance (254/254) and `node --check` on the extracted inline script before push.

2026-08-31 | Fixed dev panel dragging on mobile and off-screen positioning | Two real bugs, both traced directly from the report ('dev panel sizing isn't working smoothly... very big... not fully within the browser', 'dev panel moving also isn't working in mobile'): (1) .dev-panel-title (the drag handle) was missing touch-action:none - without it, a touch-drag there gets interpreted as the browser's own page-scroll gesture instead of delivering continuous pointermove events, the same reason the resize handles already needed it (and already had it) - dragging the panel by its title bar simply never worked on a touch device. Fixed with a new clampDevPanelPosition(), called every time applyActiveVars() runs (already fires on resize/breakpoint crossing) - re-clamps left/top to the panel's own actual current rendered size against the current viewport, so it's always fully reachable regardless of which device/width last set its position. 2026-08-31 | Click-burst shadow fixes round 2: correct pivot point, eliminated all shadow transparency | Per direct follow-up report that the earlier shadow-transform fix still wasn't right: (1) burst shadows were pivoting their skew/stretch/rotate transform around each PARTICLE's own scattered spawn point along the seam, not around the button's own center the way .shadow-caster-layer's real transform does (its default transform-origin, since it sits at inset:0 over .button-assembly) - meant every particle warped inconsistently depending on where it happened to spawn. Replaced with filter: brightness(0) invert(0.5) on both - invert(0.5) partially inverts solid black toward white, landing at solid mid-gray directly, with the silhouette's own alpha untouched (still fully opaque wherever the source art is opaque) - removed the group opacity entirely from both .shadow-caster and .click-burst-shadow-container. Two fully-opaque same-color shadows now simply occlude where they overlap instead of blending, so no combination of burst-shadow-on-burst-shadow or burst-shadow-on-main-shadow can ever darken beyond the single shared gray. 2026-08-31 | Click-burst shadow/geometry overhaul: real shadow transform, below button/base, no overlap darkening, non-overlapping spawn+flight | Per direct user feedback with an annotated screenshot, 4 separate fixes: (1) Shadow LOCATION was wrong - shadows previously used the exact same flight-path SHAPE as their real particle, just shifted by a constant pixel offset, so they read as flying from the same spot rather than being cast onto the ground like the main button/base shadow. Fixed by pre-warping the shadow's own control/end path coordinates through the SAME skew/stretch/rotate affine transform .shadow-caster-layer's CSS applies to the real button/base shadow (new transformShadowPoint() helper - scaleY -> skewX -> rotate, same composition order, matching reasoning already documented on .shadow-caster-layer), with the existing shadowXOffset/shadowYOffset kept as the transform's translate step. The sprite's own CSS transform (rotate/skewX/scaleY) is unchanged - it was already correct for 'form', only 'location' (the path itself) was missing the warp. (2) Shadows now live in a new .click-burst-shadow-container (z-index -1), separate from the real-particle container (z-index 2 still, unchanged) - previously both lived in the same z:2 container, painting above the button (z:1) though below the base (z:4); shadows now correctly sit below both. (3) No-overlap-darkening: click-burst-fly-shadow's keyframes were individually semi-transparent (opacity 0.5 while visible), so 2+ overlapping shadow particles compounded into a visibly darker patch - changed to fully opaque (1) per particle, with .click-burst-shadow-container itself carrying ONE shared group opacity (0.5, matching .shadow-caster's own), the same group-opacity technique the main shadow already used - overlapping shadows now composite as one flattened opaque shape before that single opacity applies, instead of darkening per layer. Overlap specifically between a click-burst shadow and the SEPARATE main button/base shadow (different DOM subtrees, each with its own independent group opacity) is NOT fully solved by this - flagged to the user as a smaller, harder-to-fix edge case near the button itself if it's still visible. (4) Spawn/flight overlap: replaced the previous random-angle-within-a-margin scheme with fully deterministic angle slots (each particle takes the exact center of its own equal slice of that side's zone, zero randomness in angle - a guaranteed real minimum gap, where the old margin-based approach could still land close to a neighbor), staggered origin position AND distance per slot (not randomly jittered, which could coincidentally collide), and a much smaller bezier curve-swoosh cap (was up to 0.6x distance, now 0.1-0.25x) so a wide swing can't carry a particle out of its own angular corridor into a neighbor's. This is a strong deterministic separation verified live on real bursts, not a formally proven guarantee for every possible slider/count combination - flagged honestly to the user rather than claimed as absolute. Also fixed the concurrent-particle cap to sum both containers (real + shadow), since they're no longer the same container. Verified div tag balance (205/205) and `node --check` on the extracted inline script before push.

2026-08-31 | Added an 'Overall Border' option to the 8-bit extruded text style | New checkbox (off by default) + 1 shared border color + 3 thickness sliders (shared for Start/Try Again/Round/Win-Lose, mirroring the existing depth split, plus Target's own and Speed's own) that wrap a stroke around the COMBINED text+extrusion silhouette, not just the letter glyph. There's no CSS primitive for 'outline the union of a glyph and its diagonal shadow stack', so this fakes it the way a plain text-stroke is faked with text-shadow - a ring of copies offset in 8 directions by the border thickness - but applies that ring at EVERY extrusion step (front face through the backmost step), not just once, so the ring's copies fill in the whole diagonal band's length too; a ring only at the front and back faces would leave the diagonal sides of the extrusion unbordered. The ring layers are painted first/furthest back in the text-shadow stack so the existing extrusion fill paints over them everywhere except right at the outer edge - that's what reads as a visible outline. New extrusionVars fields (overallBorderEnabled/Color/Thickness, targetOverallBorderThickness, speedOverallBorderThickness) and buildBorderRingShadow()/buildCombinedShadow() functions; existing buildExtrusionShadow() unchanged. Verified div tag balance (204/204) and `node --check` on the extracted inline script before push.

2026-08-31 | Found and fixed the real cause of the "2 frame sets" report: burst-particle shadows were rendering as an unrecognizable, differently-shaped sliver, not a darkened copy of the real particle | User provided direct evidence this time - a screen recording saved to datalog/. Since 2 prior investigations (direct function calls, then the real dropdown+click-handler path) both found zero source-mixing at the data level, went looking at what was actually on screen instead: extracted frames from the recording (via OpenCV, no ffmpeg available), found the burst moments by scanning for CLICK1's specific yellow/orange hue range restricted to the game area (a first attempt scanning generic "colorfulness" across the whole frame was scoring the dev panel's own UI, not the burst - a real dead end caught before trusting it), then zoomed into 2 separate burst clusters. Both showed the same clear pattern: the shadow wasn't a recognizable darkened CLICK1 shape, it was a completely different thin sliver/blade shape - exactly what would read as "a second, different frame set" to anyone watching, which is a materially different (and more precise) finding than the earlier "solid black could look like CLICK2" theory. Root cause: shadow particles inherited the base class's offset-rotate:auto (follow the motion path's own tangent direction, same as the real particle), then had the explicit skew(-31deg)/stretch(1.8)/rotate(41deg) shadow transform applied ON TOP - the two rotations compound, and depending on the particle's own flight angle this can stretch the shape into a barely-recognizable sliver rather than a shadow of the same shape. Fixed by giving shadow particles offset-rotate:0deg instead of auto - their POSITION still tracks the curved path exactly like before, but their own rotation no longer varies with flight direction, leaving the skew/stretch/rotate transform as the only rotation applied (uniform, undistorted, matching how the main button/base shadow already has one fixed "cast shadow" look rather than a look that changes with anything). 2026-08-30 | Fixed a real live-drag positioning bug found while testing the just-pushed dev-panel drag feature against the live site: dragging a panel (dev panel or round-breakdown panel) visually moved it at HALF the distance the cursor actually traveled | Post-push verification (this session's established practice of testing against the live deployed site, since local file:// testing here runs as a sandboxed data: URL with localStorage disabled) simulated a real drag via dispatched pointer events and read back the panel's actual getBoundingClientRect() position - not just the stored cssVars value, which is what the original round-breakdown-panel drag work earlier this session had checked. Found: cssVars was being set correctly (e.g. top=64), but the panel's live rendered position was only top:32px - exactly half. Root cause: the live-drag handlers wrote the custom property WITH a 'px' suffix (`setProperty('--x-px', newTop + 'px')`), but the CSS itself is `calc(var(--x-px) * 1px)` - designed for a bare number (matching every other cssVar's convention, see applyActiveVars(), which always calls setProperty(key, value) with the raw number). Confirmed directly via getComputedStyle readback: setting the property to the string '64px' computed to top:32px, while setting it to bare '64' correctly computed to top:64px - this browser resolves the resulting px*px mismatch to half the value rather than rejecting it outright, which is why the bug was silent rather than an obvious break. The stored cssVars values were always correct (so a reload or settings-save/load cycle would snap the panel to its true intended position, masking the bug from a values-only check) - only the LIVE visual feedback during an active drag was wrong, making both panels feel like they required dragging twice as far as intended. Fixed both drag handlers (the dev panel's, built this session, and the round-breakdown panel's, built in an earlier entry this session and carrying the identical latent bug since day one) to pass the bare number instead of a 'px'-suffixed string. [Shape: 1 real bug found through actual post-push verification against live rendered DOM state rather than assuming a values-only check was sufficient, then applied to both the new code AND a pre-existing sibling feature sharing the same root pattern; Files: 1 (index.html), 1 CHANGELOG entry; Research: none; Confidence: HIGH, directly confirmed via getComputedStyle before/after the fix]. 2026-08-30 | Found and fixed the actual structural cause of the recurring "double click" reports: showTargetAndSpeed() silently reset the visible click counter (and the debounce's timestamp history) on EVERY round transition, not just at game start, while the tap-diagnostic log never resets - meaning the two could silently disagree at any round boundary, independent of any hardware issue | User provided a much larger dataset (10 more 10-click runs, then 4 further individually-labeled runs) after the 50ms debounce fix - the aggregate picture didn't fit hardware chatter: only 1 of the ~10 "extra" occurrences showed a fast/close-position signature (correctly caught by the debounce), while the rest all had normal 175-276ms gaps indistinguishable from genuine deliberate clicks, and this held even after the user did a careful "watch the counter, stop at exactly N" test and still saw a discrepancy - ruling out both hardware chatter (wrong gap range) and simple miscounting (they were watching) as the sole explanation. Went back to the actual game-state code rather than the debounce logic (already verified working) and found showTargetAndSpeed() - which runs at the start of EVERY round, not just the game's first one - resetting totalClickCount and lastAcceptedTapTime to 0 every time, unconditionally. Since clicking is intentionally responsive "regardless of game state" (an existing, correct design choice - taps are logged/counted even before a round is actionable), any click made near a round boundary could get counted into totalClickCount and then have that count silently wiped moments later when the next round's target/speed appear, while the tap-diagnostic log (deliberately never cleared) kept every entry regardless - a structural guarantee that the two COULD diverge, not a specific timing coincidence. Re-verified the same simulated scenario post-fix: the counter now correctly stays at 1 through the round transition, and a full simulated sequence (impatient tap + round transition + 5 more taps, then a simulated win + next-round transition + more taps) shows totalClickCount and the log's own accepted-entry count staying EXACTLY equal throughout - structurally guaranteed to match now, not just observed to match in one test. No console errors; document-wide div-tag balance (177/177) unaffected. This is the first fix in this entire "double click" investigation thread backed by a mechanism that GUARANTEES the counter and log can never diverge again (a structural fix, not a probabilistic mitigation like the debounce), rather than another timing-based filter - though it's honestly disclosed that the exact reproduction (a click landing near a round boundary) wasn't confirmed to be present in every one of the user's own reported logs, only shown to be a real, confirmed, and now-eliminated class of bug.

Then applied the full settings dump as new defaults, scoped to what had actually changed from current state (most fields already matched from earlier entries): target-x/y-offset, speed-font-size/x/y-offset, and speed-color, for BOTH desktop and mobile (mobile's target/speed values differ meaningfully from desktop's in this dump, tuned independently) - updated both the JS objects and the HTML slider/color defaults. 2026-08-30 | Found and fixed the real cause of "the more/faster I click, the more it lags": the tap-diagnostic log's O(n) full re-render on every tap while its panel is open - the user tests with it open in every recording, and the array is deliberately uncapped, so the cost was growing with total taps this session, not just tap rate | User: "better but still same issue. single click looks okay. The key detail was "the more I click" (not just "the faster") - a cost that grows with TOTAL clicks so far, not just current tap RATE, meaning a fixed per-tap architecture cost (like the two shadow fixes in the previous 2 entries) couldn't fully explain it on its own; something had to be ACCUMULATING. Re-examined logTapDiagnostic(): an earlier entry already gated its expensive DOM update behind the panel being visible, but that only helps when the panel is closed - the user's own screen recordings show the tap-diagnostic panel open in every one, and the array itself is deliberately uncapped (full session history, per an explicit earlier decision) - meaning `tapDiagnostic.textContent = tapDiagnosticLog.join('\n')` was re-serializing and re-rendering the ENTIRE, ever-growing array as text on every single tap, plus forcing a synchronous layout via a `scrollHeight` read immediately after (`scrollTop = tapDiagnostic.scrollHeight`) - both real, and both scaling directly with how many taps have accumulated across the whole testing session, exactly matching the reported symptom. Fixed by making the common case append just the ONE new line (a single DOM text-node append) instead of rejoining the whole array, tracking how many entries are already reflected in the DOM (tapDiagnosticRenderedCount) so a full rebuild only happens when the DOM has genuinely fallen behind (entries logged while the panel was hidden) - wired a proactive rebuildTapDiagnosticDisplay() call into the panel's own show/hide checkbox handler so it catches up in one shot right when opened, keeping the incremental append the fast, common path on every subsequent tap. Also switched the scroll-to-bottom write from reading `scrollHeight` first (a forced synchronous reflow immediately after a text rewrite - classic layout-thrashing) to just writing `scrollTop = 1e9`, letting the browser clamp it to the actual max without an explicit read. Verified via direct measurement (not just structural checks, given this is specifically a PERFORMANCE claim): padded the log to simulate a long testing session (530, then 2030 total entries) and timed 20 real updates each way in a fair, isolated comparison (old-style full rejoin+scrollHeight-read vs new-style incremental append+scrollTop=1e9) - at ~550 entries, old averaged 6.3ms/update (worst case 12.7ms) vs new's 2.9ms average (worst case 4.7ms), a 2.2x speedup; at ~2030 entries, old averaged 12.1ms (worst case 17.2ms) vs new's 7.3ms (worst case 14.1ms), 1.7x. Honestly, the new approach isn't fully flat/O(1) either - the scroll-to-bottom write still costs something roughly proportional to total rendered content height, since the browser needs to know the true scrollable range to clamp against, so very large accumulated logs (thousands of entries) would still show SOME residual growth in cost, just meaningfully less than before. Also confirmed correctness, not just speed: a scripted test pushed 5 taps while the panel was hidden (DOM correctly stayed empty, no wasted render), then opened the panel (correctly caught up to exactly 5 rendered lines via the rebuild), then pushed 5 more while visible (correctly incremented to exactly 10 lines, matching the array length precisely) - the catch-up path and the fast path both verified working, not just one of them. 2026-08-30 | Consolidated button-shadow's two SVGs into one, swapping path geometry via JS instead of toggling which whole subtree is visible - fixes rapid-tap-specific slowness that survived the per-layer filter split | User, after confirming the per-layer filter fix worked: "better. single click looks correct. but multiple fast clicks stilll looks slow." That per-toggle cost is real and scales directly with how fast press/release cycles repeat - explaining why a single click (one toggle, plenty of frame budget) now looks correct, but rapid clicking (many toggles in quick succession) still visibly lags. Added a small JS cache (shadowButtonNormalDs/shadowButtonPressedDs, built once at load from each path's current `d` and its own `data-pressed-d`) and a `setShadowButtonPressed(pressed)` function that writes the correct 19 `d` values in one pass; wired into handleGameButtonPress/handleGameButtonRelease alongside the existing `.pressed` class toggles (which still drive the transform via CSS, unchanged). 2026-08-30 | Fixed a real lag regression caused by the previous entry's dilate filter (replaced with a free transform-based growth); widened the base-shadow's margin from 8px-uniform to a 6% scale, since the gap was "lesser but not correct" | User: "clicking animation is much laggier than before and the shadow gap still isnt fixed. its lesser, but not correct." The lag: the previous entry added `feMorphology operator="dilate"` on .shadow-caster-base-layer - a genuinely expensive per-pixel raster SVG filter primitive. Even though base-shadow's own DOM/style never changes on tap, it shares a filtered ancestor (.shadow-caster's brightness(0)+blur() filter) with button-shadow, which DOES change every tap (class toggle) - a CSS filter on a parent operates on the flattened raster of its ENTIRE subtree, so any change to a sibling forces the whole filtered group, including the expensive dilate step, to recompute. This is the same class of "shared filtered ancestor recomputes on every tap" issue this session already diagnosed and fixed once before (the shadow-layer transition removal, several entries back) - just a new instance of it, introduced by this session's own most recent change rather than being an old leftover. Fixed by removing the feMorphology filter entirely and growing base-shadow's silhouette via a plain `transform: scale()` instead - an extra multiplier folded directly into base-shadow's own transform (which no longer shares the exact shared-layer transform verbatim, now has its own copy with `* 1.06` on the scale term), GPU-composited and effectively free, no raster filter cost regardless of how often a sibling repaints. This grows base-shadow non-uniformly (proportionally more at the shape's outer extremities, less near its center) rather than by dilate's uniform pixel margin - less geometrically precise, but the press-notch sits well out toward the shadow's edge, where this kind of growth is strongest anyway. Verified: computed style confirms .shadow-caster-base-layer's filter now resolves to `none` (fully removed) and its transform's linear component is exactly 1.06x the button-shadow's own (0.741678 vs 0.699696, ratio confirmed), with the shared translate/pivot (231,50) unchanged; .game-button's transitionDuration still reads 0s (no regression there); z-index (base:1, button:0) unaffected; div-tag balance (164/164) and svg-tag balance (6/6, back down from 7 after removing the filter-holder svg) both clean; no console errors. So any REAL gap has to come from a genuine (if tiny, sub-pixel) misalignment between the base and button artwork's own boundaries that already exists in the raw, untransformed files - one that's invisible in the REAL rendering only because .base-svg-layer (z-index 4) is painted ON TOP of .game-button (z-index 1), physically covering that seam with its own opaque geometry regardless of any small drawing imprecision underneath. The shadow-caster's two layers were in the OPPOSITE paint order the whole time (base-shadow first/underneath, button-shadow second/on top, following plain DOM order) - meaning nothing was covering that native seam in the shadow, so skewX(-37deg)/scaleY(1.6)/rotate(29deg) stretched an otherwise-invisible sub-pixel gap into a visible line, independent of whatever the button-shadow's press motion was doing. This finally explains why the "freeze" fix (previous entry, zero press motion, byte-identical transforms) likely wouldn't have fully resolved it either - the gap's real cause was never the press-motion mismatch, it was the reversed paint order versus the real artwork. Fixed by giving .shadow-caster-base-layer z-index:1 and .shadow-caster-button-layer z-index:0, so base-shadow now paints on top of button-shadow exactly like .base-svg-layer paints on top of .game-button in the real rendering - a paint-order relationship that holds regardless of skew/stretch/rotation and regardless of whether button-shadow moves on press, unlike the coordinate-matching approaches tried in every previous attempt. Verified via computed-style checks in a live loaded copy: z-index reads 1 (base) / 0 (button) as set; the button-shadow's pressed-vs-resting transform now correctly differs only in its translate component (231,50 -> 178.729,73.2725) with the rotation/skew/scale component unchanged, matching the intended press motion; the SVG visibility toggle correctly swaps in both directions (#shadowButtonSvgRoot visible at rest/hidden when pressed, #shadowButtonPressedSvgRoot the reverse); real (non-shadow) button hit-testing still resolves correctly to btn-cls-6/btnp-cls-6 in both states. This is the first attempt addressing a cause the user actually confirmed (skew/stretch/rotation-related, not motion-related) rather than a working theory of my own - visual confirmation is still owed to the user's own look.

2026-08-30 | Fixed a real remaining tap-lag contributor: the button's normal/pressed SVG swap used `display: none/block`, which forces a synchronous layout reflow on every single tap - switched to `visibility: hidden/visible`, which skips that while still correctly blocking clicks on the hidden SVG | User, after the previous two entries' fixes: "the clicking animation of the button is much better than before, but there is still a slight lag." Investigated what remaining per-tap cost survives with both transitions removed and the diagnostic-log gated: `.game-button.pressed .btn-normal-svg { display: none }` / `.btn-pressed-svg { display: block }` toggles the CSS `display` property on every tap, and unlike `transform` (compositor-only) or `opacity`, changing `display` removes/re-adds the element's box from the render tree entirely, forcing the browser to recompute layout for that subtree synchronously - a real, distinct cost on top of everything fixed so far, and one that scales with how fast taps arrive since it can't be deferred to the next paint the way a compositor-only change can. Replaced both toggled rules with `visibility: hidden/visible` instead: the element keeps its computed geometry (no reflow), only paint and hit-testing are skipped for it. Confirmed hit-testing still works correctly - a `visibility: hidden` parent makes its descendants non-hit-testable even though `.game-button svg * { pointer-events: auto; }` explicitly opts them back in, because visibility (unlike pointer-events) isn't overridden by a descendant unless that descendant explicitly sets its own `visibility: visible` (nothing here does) - verified via document.elementFromPoint() at the button's visual center in both states: normal state resolves to a real path in .btn-normal-svg, forced-pressed state resolves to a real path in .btn-pressed-svg, with zero cross-contamination either direction. No console errors; div-tag balance (164/164) unaffected. Whether this closes the remaining "slight lag" the user reported, or whether some residual cost remains (e.g. the shadow-caster's parent-level blur filter recompositing on transform changes within its filtered subtree, which browsers don't always optimize to compositor-only even when the moving element's own property is just `transform`), is still owed to the user's own feel on a real tap test - this is a real, well-understood reflow fix, not a guess, but it may not be the only remaining contributor.

2026-08-30 | Fixed button/base misalignment on mobile: --button-height-offset-px and --button-press-intensity-px were fixed-pixel values applied to a vw-scaled button, so they looked proportionally huge (and pulled the button away from the base) on a narrow phone screen even though the same numbers looked reasonable on a wider desktop viewport | User sent a real screenshot from their own phone (clicko-one.vercel.app) showing a clear gap between the red button dome and the base ring below it, disconnected rather than nested together. Root cause: the button's rendered SIZE is vw-relative (--button-diameter-vw, scales with viewport width), but its vertical offset was a raw --button-height-offset-px value applied via `translateY(calc(var(...) * 1px))` - a fixed number of device-independent pixels that does NOT shrink along with the button on a narrow screen. At the diameter/viewport combination this session's own testing happened to use, -60px worked out to about -18% of the button's rendered diameter; on a phone with a much narrower viewport, that same -60px becomes a far larger percentage of the (now much smaller) button, visibly shoving it away from the base - exactly what the screenshot showed. Converted both --button-height-offset-px and --button-press-intensity-px from fixed-px semantics to percent-of-diameter semantics (`calc(var(...) * var(--button-diameter-vw) / 100 * 1vw)` in place of `calc(var(...) * 1px)`), so the button's recess depth and press-animation travel now stay proportional to its own on-screen size at every viewport width instead of only being correct at whichever width they happened to be tuned on. Reset the defaults to -5 (height) and 3 (press intensity) under the new percent-of-diameter scale, and rescaled the dev-panel sliders' range/step to match (-30..30 / 0..30, step 0.5, was -60..60 / 0..60 step 1) - the -5/3 starting values are a best-effort visual estimate from the screenshot (a real gap, closed by eye, not measured precisely from a photo), not a scientifically exact figure; fine-tuning further via the sliders (now that they scale consistently everywhere) is exactly what they're for. Verified via computed-geometry checks at BOTH a desktop-width (890px) and mobile-width (375px) viewport: the ratio of the button's actual translateY offset to its own rendered diameter now reads exactly -0.05 at both widths (previously this ratio would have differed sharply between the two, which was the entire bug) - confirming the fix holds the same visual proportion regardless of screen size. 2026-08-30 | Implemented a genuinely scalable shadow: added a dedicated shadow-caster layer (a flattened, blurred duplicate of the artwork, resized via a real transform:scale()) since CSS drop-shadow() has no spread/size parameter at all - the two prior entries' attempts (multiplying/adding to blur) were always going to be an approximation, not what was actually asked for | User: "the shadow size is not blur related. it is literally a scaling smaller of the shadow itself" - correctly rejecting both prior attempts, since neither actually resizes the shadow's silhouette, only its softness. CSS's `filter: drop-shadow()` genuinely has no spread/size parameter (unlike box-shadow's spread-radius) - there's no way to make an existing drop-shadow's footprint bigger or smaller without either a spread-capable shadow technique (not available for arbitrary SVG alpha shapes) or an actual separate, independently-sized copy of the shape. Implemented the latter: a new `.shadow-caster` element containing duplicate copies of the base+button SVG artwork, generated via a Python script (reads the ALREADY-CORRECT inlined SVGs straight out of index.html and renames every id/class/clip-path reference with a shadow-specific prefix - shadowButtonSvgRoot, shbtn-cls-*, shbase-cls-*, shbtn-clippath) rather than hand-duplicating ~20KB of path data, avoiding any repeat of the earlier transcription-drop bug and guaranteeing the shadow always matches the real artwork exactly. This layer sits behind both real layers (z-index 0, below the button's z-index 1), is flattened to a solid dark silhouette and blurred via `filter: brightness(0) blur()`, and is positioned/sized via `transform: translate(shadow-x, shadow-y) scale(shadow-scale)` - translate for position (matches the X/Y sliders exactly as before), and a REAL `scale()` for size, which actually resizes the shadow's rendered footprint rather than approximating size via blur. Removed the old `filter: drop-shadow()` from .button-assembly entirely, replaced by this layer. Reset the Shadow Size slider back to genuine scale-multiplier semantics (0.1-3x, default 1 = same size as the object) now that it drives a real transform instead of blur math. Also increased the Shadow X slider's range to +-300px (was +-200) and set the default to 250 per request. Verified via computed-style/DOM checks in a live loaded copy of index.html: no id collisions between the real artwork and its shadow duplicate (each shadow-prefixed id appears exactly once), the caster's computed filter/transform read correctly, its bounding-box WIDTH measured via getBoundingClientRect() doubles exactly (333.75px -> 667.5px) when Shadow Size goes from 1 to 2 - confirming a true, precise 2x scale rather than an approximation - click hit-testing at the button's visual center still resolves to the real interactive artwork (not the inert shadow clone, which has pointer-events:none confirmed all the way down to its individual path elements), and a dispatched pointerdown still increments the click counter normally. 2026-08-28 | CORRECTION: position+time debounce (previous entry) was defaulted OFF after real user data showed it was rejecting genuine taps, not just the hardware duplicate | User ran 5 more "5 taps" reproductions with the 250ms/3px filter active; every single test showed exactly 1 real tap rejected (4/5 registered) and, critically, the user explicitly confirmed by direct observation: "no i intended 5 taps, so its counting 1 less than intended" -- a false positive, not a correctly-filtered duplicate. This falsifies the prior entry's assumption that near-identical position reliably marks a hardware duplicate: a practiced player naturally holds the mouse still while repeatedly tapping a static on-screen target, so genuinely separate real presses CAN legitimately land within a few pixels of each other, indistinguishable from a duplicate by position alone. There is no software signal available that can tell these apart with certainty. Since a filter that silently eats a real tap causes a false loss -- objectively worse than occasionally letting a genuine hardware duplicate through, which a player can actually notice -- changed TAP_DEBOUNCE_MS's default from 250 to 0 (effectively off: every tap counts) and updated the dev-panel slider's default display to match. The position+time filtering logic itself is left fully intact and still available -- a player who specifically knows their own mouse double-fires can dial the slider back up and accept that tradeoff themselves, rather than it being silently imposed as the default for everyone. Also fixed a false alarm while re-reading the touched function: two comment lines appeared as a bare backslash instead of "//" in a Grep tool's rendered output, which looked like a JS syntax error at first glance -- confirmed via direct file Read that the actual file content was correct ("//" throughout, no bug), so no code fix was needed there beyond updating the comment text itself to describe the new off-by-default behavior. Not independently re-verified against a live browser session this round (no code path changed beyond the default constant and its label) -- the fix is a 1-line default-value change to logic already verified correct in the prior entry's own tests.

2026-08-28 | ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED: "1 click sometimes logs as 2" is a genuine mouse-hardware double-click fault, confirmed by real user data | 3rd real reproduction, now conclusive: entries 4 and 5 of a 6-entry log shared the EXACT same position (802,337)/pointerId=1/isPrimary=true, 142ms apart, while every other consecutive pair showed natural 1-8px jitter -- textbook signature of a worn mouse microswitch reporting one physical press as two electrical signals (a well-documented class of hardware fault, historically nicknamed the "Logitech double-click bug" though not manufacturer-specific), not a software or logic issue. A pure time-based debounce turned out to be unsafe here: this exact user's own legitimate tap gaps ran as low as 165ms, only ~20ms above the 142ms duplicate delay -- any single threshold wide enough to reliably catch the fault would also reject real fast taps once the game's own difficulty demands them. Fixed by requiring BOTH a short gap AND near-identical position (<=3px) before rejecting -- position is the signal that actually discriminates a hardware duplicate (same pixel, since the mouse didn't move) from a genuinely separate fast tap (which shows jitter even when a player tries to hold still). Default debounce window widened 50->250ms since it's now safely position-gated; kept tunable via the dev panel (relabeled "Debounce Window (ms, same pos only)") since the exact fault characteristics are hardware-specific to the player's device. Verified via 2 precisely state-controlled tests (bypassing this environment's own async-timing throttling by setting lastAcceptedTapTime/X/Y directly rather than trusting real sleep delays): the exact reported duplicate pattern (same pos, 142ms gap) -> correctly rejected; an otherwise-identical tap 5px away at the same 142ms gap -> correctly accepted, confirming real fast taps with natural jitter survive.

2026-08-28 | Dev panel hide (X) button + persistent reopen toggle - fixes "can't hide it on mobile browser" | The panel only ever had the D-key toggle, which doesn't exist on a touchscreen. Added an X button in the panel's own header (calls the same hide logic) plus a small always-present "DEV" button fixed in the bottom-right corner of the screen that toggles the panel open/closed regardless of its current state - without this second piece, hiding the panel on mobile would have been a one-way trip with no way back. Both gated behind the existing isDevAllowed check (file://data:/localhost/?dev=1) so neither ever appears for a real player on a deployed URL. Verified via direct clicks: the X button hides the panel, the persistent toggle correctly reopens it.

2026-08-28 | Tap log now captures position/pointerId/isPrimary - real user data (12 logged vs 10 physical clicks, all gaps 135-170ms) ruled out both prior duplicate-detection theories | Live user reproduction with the previous diagnostic gave hard evidence: gaps between ALL logged entries were 135-170ms, consistent with normal human tap rhythm, not the sub-50ms pattern the TAP_DEBOUNCE_MS hardware-bounce check targets, nor the ~300ms delayed pattern the touch-compat check targets - neither existing detector could have caught this even in principle, so widening either threshold further would have been guessing blind. User separately confirmed it only happens when clicking fast, pointing toward a genuine input-hardware artifact (trackpad/mouse struggling with rapid successive contacts) over a pure logic bug. Added the one signal that can actually distinguish a software-duplicated event (same x/y, same pointerId) from a genuinely separate physical contact (different x/y and/or a new pointerId, e.g. a trackpad momentarily registering a second point): logTapDiagnostic now threads the real PointerEvent through and records pos=(x,y)/id=pointerId/primary=isPrimary alongside the existing type/gap fields. Caught mid-fix that the user's first retest happened against an intermediate save (log format added before the event object was fully threaded through both call sites), which is why it showed 'pos=(?,?)' -- completed the wiring and verified a real click now populates real coordinates/id/primary before asking for another reproduction.

2026-08-28 | Click counter/tap log repositioned next to the button, now count from before Round 1 (resetting each round), full unlimited scrollable history + copy button | (1) Reversed the earlier "counter only counts once Round 1 starts" behavior per explicit request -- totalClickCount/tap-log now increment on every accepted press regardless of game state (landing-page clicks count too), while the actual gameplay tap-counting logic (currentTapCount, win/lose) stays properly gated behind isPlaying/canTap so landing-page taps still can't affect a round. The copy button repeated the earlier localStorage lesson: its clipboard-unavailable fallback called window.prompt(), which itself throws uncaught in this environment's sandboxed preview -- rewrote to a defensive chain (Clipboard API -> textarea+execCommand('copy') fallback -> button-text "FAILED" feedback) with every branch wrapped, verified it no longer throws and gives visible button-text feedback ("COPIED!"/"FAILED") instead of a blocking dialog that isn't universally supported. 2026-08-27 | Follow-up: user confirmed the 50ms debounce alone didn't fully fix "1 click sometimes logs as 2" (testing on desktop); added pointerType-aware touch-compat detection plus a live diagnostic instead of guessing a 3rd number | Since the user is on desktop (confirmed), the touch->mouse synthetic-compatibility-event theory doesn't apply to their case, but was still added defensively for future mobile players: a mouse-type pointerdown arriving <600ms after an accepted touch-type one is now also rejected (that specific browser quirk fires its duplicate ~300ms later, well outside the 50ms bounce-only window, so it wouldn't have been the desktop fix anyway). For the actual desktop report, rather than guess whether 50ms is too tight for their specific mouse/trackpad's bounce characteristics, added a live on-screen tap diagnostic (piggybacks on the existing "Show Click Counter" dev-panel checkbox) that logs the pointerType, exact gap in ms, and accept/reject outcome for each of the last 6 tap attempts -- so the next reproduction gives real measured data instead of another blind guess. 2026-08-27 | Debounced duplicate pointerdown events - fixes "a single click sometimes logs as 2" | User: fast clicking sometimes registered one physical press as 2 taps. Most likely cause: mouse/trackpad switch "bounce" (a worn or cheap switch sending 2 electrical down-signals for 1 physical press, a well-known hardware phenomenon) or an occasional duplicate pointerdown from the browser - neither is a code logic bug, both are genuine duplicate events arriving a few ms apart. Added a 50ms debounce: a pointerdown within 50ms of the last ACCEPTED one is ignored outright (no real intentional 2nd tap is ever that fast; only bounce/duplicate events are). Reset alongside the other per-round state so it can't carry over between rounds. Verified: 2 pointerdown events dispatched synchronously back-to-back (genuinely 0ms apart, a closer proxy for real switch bounce than a throttled setTimeout, which this test tool's tab throttling inflated from a requested 5ms to an actual 350ms on the first attempt) -> counted as exactly 1 tap; 5 taps spaced a real 300ms apart -> all 5 counted normally, confirming the debounce doesn't interfere with legitimate gameplay.

2026-08-27 | Clickable area restricted to the button's actual painted pixels; click counter now only counts from Round 1; button no longer casts its own shadow (base's is the only one) | (1) .game-button and its svg root set to pointer-events:none, with `.game-button svg *` re-enabled to auto -- SVG shapes default to visiblePainted hit-testing, so only the button.svg's actual vector fills are clickable now, not its full rectangular bounding box (which was mostly empty margin around the artwork). First attempt set pointer-events:auto directly on the svg root, which made its whole rectangular canvas clickable (verified via document.elementFromPoint at a corner returning the raw <svg> element) -- fixed by pushing auto down to descendant shapes instead and leaving the root at none. (2) totalClickCount++ moved below the isPlaying/canTap guard -- the visual press feedback (the .pressed class swap) still fires unconditionally so the button stays responsive pre-game, but the debug counter itself no longer increments for landing-page presses. (3-4) Removed the drop-shadow() from the button svg's filter entirely (kept only hue-rotate) -- base was already the frontmost, most-visible layer and its own drop-shadow was sufficient on its own; having both cast independent shadows was the "2 overlapped" the user flagged. Verified all 3 fixes live: a real click at the button's bounding-box corner (empty svg margin) registered 0 taps, an identical click at the visible button dome registered 1; a click before START stayed at totalClickCount=0; computed styles confirmed the button svg's filter has no drop-shadow while base's still does.

2026-08-27 | CORE SEMANTICS CORRECTION: the timing value is a MAXIMUM gap between taps, not a minimum floor -- root cause of every prior "win/lose doesn't work" report this session | User gave 3 precise repro scenarios (stop early = nothing happens when it should lose; hit exact target = nothing happens when it should win; overshoot = nothing happens when it should lose) plus an explicit correction: "minimum time means maximum time... there is a maximum time, which is the top margin of how much time is allowed between each click." This is the actual root cause of every timing bug chased this session -- from the very start, the "minimum time between taps" reading of the original spec was implemented as a FLOOR (reject taps arriving faster than the value, silently, no penalty), when it should have been a CEILING (a maximum allowed gap; no floor at all, tap as fast as you want). That floor logic silently dropped most of a normal player's real taps whenever they tapped faster than the current value, so the internal counter could get permanently stuck under the target no matter how many times they physically pressed -- exactly matching "I clicked the right amount of times... nothing happens" and "I clicked more than enough... nothing happens" (both undercounted internally). Rebuilt the mechanic to the user's literal 3-part spec: every tap counts immediately, no speed floor; an overshoot (count > target) ends the round in an instant loss; every tap (whether under, at, or count-wise irrelevant to the target) reschedules a single maxTimeMs countdown, and if no further tap arrives before it fires, the round resolves immediately based on whatever the count is then (win if exactly target, lose otherwise) -- this also fixes the "stop early, nothing happens" report, since the prior version (this same session, 2 commits ago) had removed all timing checks for taps below the target based on a since-corrected reading of an earlier, less precise description. Renamed minSpeedMs -> maxTimeMs and relabeled the dev panel/on-screen display ("Starting Speed" -> "Starting Max Time (ms)", "Speed Display (min ms)" -> "Speed Display (max ms between taps)") so the code and UI can no longer silently drift from the corrected semantics the way the old naming did. Verified via 3 dispatched-PointerEvent tests matching the user's own 3 reported scenarios exactly: 3 taps against target=8 then a 600ms pause -> "DIDN'T MAKE IT! (3/8)" (was previously silent); 5 fast taps (60ms apart) against target=5 -> "NICE!" (was previously silent, since the taps would have been rejected as "too fast" under the old floor logic); 7 taps against target=4 -> instant "ONE TOO FAR! (5/4)" the moment the 5th tap registered.

2026-08-27 | Win/loss mechanic rebuilt from scratch to match the user's exact description, replacing the whole "auto-check after every tap" model | User: "the winning still doesnt work... theres a score counter, so when the score counter hits the right number, you start counting and if he doesnt press anything by the time the minimum time has passed, he wins. if he does hit it again, then immediately tell him he lost... I dont want to have to wait for the round to end." Follow-up clarifying the ORIGINAL symptom: "i tapped the button the correct amount of times, but nothing happened, then i waited for 2 seconds, then it told me i lost" -- a correct exact-match tap sequence resolving to a LOSS after a delay, not a premature loss. Root cause: every previous version of this mechanic (including this same session's earlier stopGraceMs fix) scheduled a generic pass/fail check after EVERY tap, comparing count to target only when that timer fired later -- any code path or timing edge in that shared generic check could in principle resolve an exact match to endGame(false) instead of endGame(true), and there was no structural guarantee against it. Rebuilt entirely: taps below the target now carry NO timer at all (pause as long as you want between them, only the min-gap floor applies when a tap does happen); reaching the target starts a dedicated win-only countdown whose callback can ONLY ever call endGame(true) or do nothing -- there is no code path left, anywhere, that can produce a delayed loss for an exact-match sequence. Once the target is reached, ANY further tap (even one fast enough that it would normally be ignored pre-target) ends the round in an immediate loss with zero wait, matching "I dont want to have to wait." Removed the now-fully-unused Stop-Tap Grace slider/state. Verified via 3 dispatched-PointerEvent tests: (1) reach target, wait past minSpeedMs with no more taps -> win ("YES!"), (2) reach target, tap again after only 50ms -> instant loss ("ONE TOO FAR! (3/3)", ~1075ms total elapsed, no waiting), (3) tap some taps, pause 3s mid-sequence (well past the old grace window), resume and reach target -> win ("FANTASTIC!"), confirming pausing before the target is no longer penalized at all.

2026-08-27 | Unified mouse+touch input to Pointer Events (eliminates a possible double-tap-count path); loss messages now say why and show the actual count | User reported "still not working" (correct tap count still rejected) after the prior round's fixes -- re-audited the input layer rather than re-trusting the earlier timing fix alone: gameButton/startButton each had BOTH mousedown/mouseup/mouseleave AND touchstart/touchend listeners attached simultaneously, relying on preventDefault() to fully suppress the synthetic mouse events a real touch fires afterward. That suppression is standard but not 100% guaranteed across every mobile browser/WebView, so a real device where it doesn't fully hold could double-count a single physical tap (touchstart fires the count, a leaked synthetic mousedown fires it again) -- exactly consistent with a player's real tap count reading correct to them while the game silently registers more. Replaced both elements' separate mouse+touch listener pairs with unified pointerdown/pointerup/pointercancel/pointerleave (Pointer Events merge mouse/touch/pen into one stream with no synthetic-event duplication by design). Verified via dispatched PointerEvents: 5 rapid taps against a target of 3 correctly stopped counting at exactly 4 (immediate overshoot-loss, no double-count), a 3-tap/5-target timeout case correctly landed on exactly 3 | Loss messages: split the single generic pool into 'too many' (TOO MANY!/OVERSHOT!/TOO FAR!/etc) and 'not enough' (TOO SLOW!/NOT ENOUGH!/RAN OUT OF TIME!/etc) pools selected by the actual failure cause, and appended the real (count/target) to every loss message -- e.g. "TOO FAR! (4/3)" -- both to directly answer the user's ask and to make any future report immediately diagnosable from the screen itself rather than needing a live debugging session.

2026-08-27 | Dev panel gated to local/?dev=1 access; mobile hardening including a real touch-start bug fix | Dev panel (and its D-key toggle) now only reachable via file://, data:, localhost/127.0.0.1, or a ?dev=1 query param -- a real deployed URL never shows it to players; kept in the DOM (not removed) since game logic reads its slider values directly, only visibility/toggle access is gated. Verified all 4 access-decision branches (public no param, public with ?dev=1, public with an unrelated param, localhost) via direct boolean checks | While auditing touch handling, found and fixed a real mobile-breaking bug: the START button's actual game-start logic lived in a 'click' listener, but touchstart's preventDefault() (added earlier to suppress the iOS/Android double-tap-to-zoom gesture) also suppresses the synthetic click a touch would otherwise fire afterward -- meaning START would never actually start the game on a real touchscreen, only show its press animation. Fixed by extracting the start logic into startGame() and calling it explicitly from touchend too. Added touch-action:manipulation and a stricter viewport meta (maximum-scale=1, user-scalable=no) to both tappable buttons to prevent pinch/double-tap-zoom from interfering with rapid tapping. Verified layout and no console errors at a real 375x812 mobile viewport; verified the startGame() fix's own logic directly (a raw .click() call correctly starts the round) after the emulated-touch tap in this specific test tool didn't register -- most likely a coordinate/event-simulation quirk of the test tool's mobile emulation rather than a real bug, given the underlying logic checks out; flagged to the user as worth one real-device confirmation.

2026-08-27 | Real root cause of the "correct tap count still loses" report found and fixed; shadow architecture rebuilt; defaults overhauled | Diagnosed via instrumented live logging (not guessing): the round-end auto-check fired minSpeedMs+stopGraceMs after each tap with only a 200ms default grace window, so any natural human pause between taps easily exceeded it and ended the round early with a truthful-looking but misleadingly low tap count -- widened default stopGraceMs 200->900ms (slider range 0-500 -> 0-2000ms) and confirmed via a clean 4-tap/1000ms-spacing test that it now correctly wins where it previously always died after tap #1.
